[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}\hookrightarrow MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})
and

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}\hookrightarrow MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})\|\lesssim\rho\gamma_{o}^{-m_{o}}.

By
C.6
, since
\frac{d_{o}}{2s_{o}}<1
and
\frac{d_{z}}{2s_{z}}<1
, we have
MW^{\theta m_{z},\theta m_{o}}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})
, with embedding norm a universal constant independent of
n
. By Lemma
C.5
, we thus have
(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}),[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})})_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})
and

\displaystyle\|(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}),[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})})_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})\|\lesssim\rho^{\theta}\gamma_{o}^{-\theta m_{o}},

Next, since we know by Assumption
2.2
that
P_{ZO}
is equivalent to Lebesgue measure on
\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}
,
C.4
shows that we have

\displaystyle\left(L^{2}(P_{ZO}),[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\right)_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow\left(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}),[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}\right)_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(P_{ZO}),

and we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left(L^{2}(P_{ZO}),[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\right)_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(P_{ZO})\right\|\lesssim\rho^{\theta}\gamma_{o}^{-\theta m_{o}}

(64)

Finally, by
Steinwart and Scovel 2012
[Theorem 4.6],
(L^{2}(P_{ZO}),[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})})_{\theta,2}\cong[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]^{\theta}_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}
with the constant of equivalence depends only on
\theta
. Putting it back to Eq. (
64
) completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Lemma C.3
.

Suppose Assumption
2.2
holds, and let
m_{z},m_{o},\rho
be as defined in the statement of Assumption
2.2
. We have
[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}\hookrightarrow MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})
with

\displaystyle\left\|[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}\hookrightarrow MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})\right\|\lesssim\rho\gamma_{o}^{-m_{o}}.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{H}_{FO}\hookrightarrow L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})
denote the canonical inclusion map. Since we have
(\ker\mathrm{id})^{\perp}\cong[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}
, we may represent an arbitrary element of
[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}
as
[f]
, where
f\in(\ker\mathrm{id})^{\perp}
. Moreover, we have
\|[f]\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}=\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}
. We fix
f
for the remainder of the proof.

Let
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{d_{z}}
with
|\bm{\alpha}|\leq m_{z}
and
\bm{\beta}\in\mathbb{N}^{d_{o}}
with
|\bm{\beta}|\leq m_{o}
, we have for any
f\in\mathcal{H}_{FO}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}f\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R})}=\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\partial_{\mathbf{z}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}f(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\partial_{\mathbf{z}}^{\bm{\alpha}}\partial_{\mathbf{o}}^{\bm{\beta}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sum_{\bm{\beta}_{1}+\bm{\beta}_{2}=\bm{\beta}}\binom{\bm{\beta}}{\bm{\beta}_{1}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}_{\mathbf{o}}w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{2}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sum_{\bm{\beta}_{1}+\bm{\beta}_{2}=\bm{\beta}}\binom{\bm{\beta}}{\bm{\beta}_{1}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}_{\mathbf{o}}w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{2}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\bm{\beta}_{1}+\bm{\beta}_{2}=\beta}\binom{\bm{\beta}}{\bm{\beta}_{1}}\right)^{2}\left(\max_{\bm{\beta}_{2}\leq\beta}\sup_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}}\left|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{2}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{2}\right)\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\max_{\bm{\beta}_{1}\leq\beta}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}_{\mathbf{o}}w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\bm{\beta}_{1}+\bm{\beta}_{2}=\beta}\binom{\bm{\beta}}{\bm{\beta}_{1}}\right)^{2}\left(\max_{\bm{\beta}_{2}\leq\beta}\sup_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}}\left|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{2}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{2}\right)\max_{\bm{\beta}_{1}\leq\beta}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}_{\mathbf{o}}w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle c_{m_{o},d_{o}}\left(\max_{\bm{\beta}_{2}\leq\beta}\sup_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}}\left|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{2}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{2}\right)\gamma_{o}^{-2|\beta|}\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{m_{o},d_{o}}\left(\max_{\bm{\alpha}\leq m_{z}}\max_{\bm{\beta}\leq m_{o}}\sup_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}}\left|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{2}\right)\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}}\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2},

where
(a)
follows from the fact that, for any
f\in\mathcal{H}_{FO}
, there exists
w\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
such that
f(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})=\langle w,F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}
and
\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}=\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}
.

(b)
follows from generalized Leibniz’s rule and differentiation under the integral sign
Klenke 2013
[Theorem 6.28],
(c)
follows from a Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

(d)
follows from the following arguments.

From
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
, we know that
for any
w\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
, there exists
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
such that

\displaystyle w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})=\langle g,\Phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})\Phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})},\quad\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}=\|g\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})},

with
\Phi_{\gamma_{x}}:\mathcal{X}\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}),\Phi_{\gamma_{o}}:\mathcal{O}\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})
defined in
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
.
We have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\partial^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}_{\mathbf{o}}w(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\partial_{\mathbf{o}}^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}g(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{o}^{\prime})\Phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\Phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})(\mathbf{o}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{O}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}g(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{o}^{\prime})\Phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\partial_{\mathbf{o}}^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}(\Phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})(\mathbf{o}^{\prime}))\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}\left(\Phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\partial_{\mathbf{o}}^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}(\Phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})(\mathbf{o}^{\prime}))\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}(\Phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}))^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}}\left(\partial_{\mathbf{o}}^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}(\Phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})(\mathbf{o}^{\prime}))\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\int_{\mathcal{O}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}}\left(\partial_{\mathbf{o}}^{\bm{\beta}_{1}}(\Phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})(\mathbf{o}^{\prime}))\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}

\displaystyle\|g\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}c_{\bm{\beta}_{1},d_{o}}\gamma_{o}^{-2|\bm{\beta}_{1}|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|w\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}c_{\bm{\beta}_{1},d_{o}}\gamma_{o}^{-2|\bm{\beta}_{1}|},

where we’re allowed to exchange differentiation and integration in
(i)
using the differentiation lemma
Klenke 2013
[Theorem 6.28],
(ii)
follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}(\Phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}))^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\left(\frac{2^{d_{x}/2}}{\pi^{d_{x}/4}\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}/2}}\exp\left(-2\frac{\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\|^{2}_{2}}{\gamma_{x}^{2}}\right)\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}=1,

and
(iii)
follows from the proof of
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
[Theorem 4.48]. Here
c_{\bm{\beta}_{1},d_{o}}
is a constant only depending on
\bm{\beta}_{1},d_{o}
. Hence we’ve shown

\displaystyle\|f\|^{2}_{MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\alpha}\leq m_{z},\bm{\beta}\leq m_{o}}\left\|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}f\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{\alpha}\leq m_{z},\bm{\beta}\leq m_{o}}\left(\max_{\bm{\alpha}\leq m_{z}}\max_{\bm{\beta}\leq m_{o}}\sup_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}}\left|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{2}\right)\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}}\|w\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{\alpha\leq m_{z},\bm{\beta}\leq m_{o}}1\right)\left(\max_{\bm{\alpha}\leq m_{z}}\max_{\bm{\beta}\leq m_{o}}\sup_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}}\left|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{2}\right)\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}}\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}}\|[f]\|_{[\mathcal{H}_{FO}]_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}}^{2}.

The second last inequality holds by Assumption
2.2
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

C.3
Auxiliary results for
Appendix

C

Lemma C.4
.

Let
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}
be two measures on
\mathcal{Z}
which are equivalent, i.e
0<c^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu_{2}}{\mathrm{d}\nu_{1}}\leq c<\infty
.
Let
\mathcal{H}\subseteq L^{2}(\nu_{1})
be a Hilbert space. Then
(L^{2}(\nu_{1}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow(L^{2}(\nu_{2}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}
with embedding norm
\|(L^{2}(\nu_{1}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow(L^{2}(\nu_{2}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}\|\leq c^{1-\theta}
.

Proof.

For any
f\in(L^{2}(\nu_{1}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\left(L^{2}(\nu_{2}),\mathcal{H}\right)_{\theta,2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}K(t,x;L^{2}(\nu_{2}),\mathcal{H})\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}\inf_{y\in\mathcal{H}}\left\{\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\nu_{2})}+t\|y\|_{\mathcal{H}}\right\}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(ct^{-\theta}\inf_{y\in\mathcal{H}}\left\{\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\nu_{1})}+\frac{t}{c}\|y\|_{\mathcal{H}}\right\}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}c^{1-\theta}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}\inf_{y\in\mathcal{H}}\left\{\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\nu_{1})}+t\|y\|_{\mathcal{H}}\right\}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=c^{1-\theta}\|f\|_{\left(L^{2}(\nu_{1}),\mathcal{H}\right)_{\theta,2}}

In the above derivations, we use
\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\nu_{2})}\leq c\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\nu_{1})}
in
(a)
, and the change of variables
t\mapsto ct
in
(b)
.
∎

Lemma C.5
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}=[0,1]^{d}
. Let
\mathcal{H},W\subseteq L^{2}(\mathcal{Z})
be two Hilbert spaces. Suppose that
\mathcal{H}\hookrightarrow W
with embedding norm
\|\mathcal{H}\hookrightarrow W\|\leq c
. Suppose that
\theta\in(0,1)
is chosen so that
\left(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W\right)_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z})
holds. Then we have
\left(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),\mathcal{H}\right)_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z})
with embedding norm
\leq c^{\theta}c_{0}
, where
c_{0}
only depends on
\theta,m
and
d_{z}
.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
Kanagawa et al. 2018
[Lemma A.2, Corollary 4.13].
We have, for any
x\in(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W)_{\theta,2}
,

\displaystyle\|x\|_{\left(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W\right)_{\theta,2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}K\left(t,x;L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W\right)\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}\inf_{y\in W}\left\{\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z})}+t\|y\|_{W}\right\}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}\inf_{y\in\mathcal{H}}\left\{\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z})}+t\|y\|_{W}\right\}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}\inf_{y\in\mathcal{H}}\left\{\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z})}+tc\|y\|_{\mathcal{H}}\right\}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=c^{\theta}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\left(tc\right)^{-\theta}\inf_{y\in\mathcal{H}}\left\{\|x-y\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z})}+tc\|y\|_{\mathcal{H}}\right\}\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=c^{\theta}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(t^{-\theta}K\left(t,x;L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),\mathcal{H}\right)\right)^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=c^{\theta}\|x\|_{\left(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),\mathcal{H}\right)_{\theta,2}}.

Notice that we correct an error in
Kanagawa et al. 2018
which obtained the exponent
-\theta
instead of
\theta
due to an error in the change of variables argument in
(a)
.
Now we have proved that
(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W)_{\theta,2}
and
\left\|(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W)_{\theta,2}\right\|\leq c^{\theta}
.
On the other hand, by assumption, we have
(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W)_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z})
. Consequently, we have
(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),\mathcal{H})_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow W(L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}),W)_{\theta,2}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z})
. The embedding norm is bounded by
c^{\theta}c_{0}
, where
c_{0}
depends only on
\theta
,
m
, and
d_{z}
.
∎

Lemma C.6
.

If
\frac{d_{o}}{2s_{o}}<1
and
\frac{d_{z}}{2s_{z}}<1
, then the dominating mixed smoothness Sobolev space
MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}([0,1]^{d_{o}+d_{z}})
continuously embeds into
L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d_{o}+d_{z}})
.

Proof.

See
[
Schmeisser 2007
, Equation 1.13]
for the result where the domain is
\mathbb{R}^{d_{z}+d_{o}}
. By
DeVore and Sharpley 1993
, there exists a continuous extension operator
\mathcal{E}:MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}([0,1]^{d_{o}+d_{z}})\to MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}+d_{z}})
such that
\|\mathcal{E}[f]\|_{MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}+d_{z}})}\leq C^{\prime}\|f\|_{MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}([0,1]^{d_{o}+d_{z}})}
holds for some universal constant
C^{\prime}
.
Hence, for
f\in MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}([0,1]^{d_{o}+d_{z}})
, we thus have
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]^{d_{o}+d_{z}})}\leq\|\mathcal{E}[f]\|_{L^{\infty}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}+d_{z}})\leq C\|\mathcal{E}[f]\|_{MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}+d_{z}})}\leq C^{\prime}\|f\|_{MW^{s_{o},s_{z}}_{2}([0,1]^{d_{o}+d_{z}})}
, for some universal constants
C,C^{\prime}>0
.
∎

Lemma C.7
.

Let
\mathcal{Z}=[0,1]^{d_{z}}
and
\mathcal{O}=[0,1]^{d_{o}}
. The interpolation space
[L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}),W_{2}^{s_{z}}(\mathcal{Z})\otimes W_{2}^{s_{o}}(\mathcal{O})]_{\theta,2}\cong W_{2}^{s_{z}\theta}(\mathcal{Z})\otimes W_{2}^{s_{o}\theta}(\mathcal{O})
.

Proof.

The proof follows immediately from
Defant and Michels 2000
.
∎

Lemma C.8
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be the vector-valued RKHS of the operator-valued kernel defined in Eq. (
4
) with
k_{Z},k_{O,1}
being Sobolev reproducing kernels of smoothness
t_{z},t_{o}
. Then,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{G}&\overset{(a)}{\cong}\mathcal{H}_{X}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Z}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{O,1}\overset{(b)}{\simeq}\mathcal{H}_{X}\otimes W^{t_{z}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z};\mathbb{R})\otimes W^{t_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R})\\
&\overset{(c)}{\cong}\mathcal{H}_{X}\otimes MW^{t_{z},t_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R})\overset{(d)}{\cong}MW^{t_{z},t_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X}),\end{aligned}

(65)

Proof.

(a)
is proved in
Li et al. 2024a
,
(b)
holds by definition of Sobolev reproducing kernels,
(c)
is proved in
Aubin 2011
and
(d)
is an extension of
Aubin 2011
.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
4.1
in the main text

We first construct the auxiliary RKHS function
f_{\mathrm{aux}}
defined in Eq. (
40
) in the main text, and give an upper bound on
\|f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}
. Let
r=\max\{\lfloor s_{x}\rfloor,\lfloor s_{o}\rfloor\}+1
. For
\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{d})
, we define an
approximate identity

[
Giné and Nickl 2021
, Section 4.1.2]

K_{\bm{\gamma}}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
via

\displaystyle K_{\mathbf{1}}(\mathbf{x}):=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\frac{1}{j^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{x_{i}^{2}}{j^{2}}\right),\quad K_{\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{x}):=K_{\mathbf{1}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{x}}{\bm{\gamma}}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}

(66)

Note that Eq. (
66
) reduces to
Eberts and Steinwart 2013
[Eq. (8)] when
\gamma_{1}=\dots=\gamma_{d}
,
s_{1}=\dots,s_{d}
.
We define
K_{\gamma_{x}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\to\mathbb{R}
to be
K_{\bm{\gamma}_{x}}
with
\bm{\gamma}_{x}=\left[\gamma_{x},\ldots,\gamma_{x}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}
(note this agrees with Eq. (
36
) in the main) and

K_{\gamma_{o}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}\to\mathbb{R}
to be
K_{\bm{\gamma}_{o}}
with
\bm{\gamma}_{o}=\left[\gamma_{o},\ldots,\gamma_{o}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}
.
Define
\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\to\mathbb{R}
as an indicator function of
\{\bm{\omega}_{x}:\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|\leq\gamma_{x}^{-1}\}
.

By the Young’s Convolution Inequality and the fact that
\mathcal{F}
is unitary, we have

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\ast}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\leq\|f_{\ast}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\|\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}

The right hand side is finite by
2.3
in the main text. Hence we apply the Fourier operator
\mathcal{F}
to
f_{\ast}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]
to find that,
(\forall\mathbf{o}\in\mathcal{O})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[f_{\ast}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]]=\mathcal{F}[f_{\ast}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})]\cdot\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}.

Therefore,
f_{\ast}\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]=f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}}
where
f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}}
is defined in Eq. (
38
). We define
f_{\ast,\mathrm{high}}=f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}}
. Then we have,
(\forall\mathbf{o}\in\mathcal{O})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[f_{\ast,\mathrm{high}}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})]=\mathcal{F}[f_{\ast}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})]-\mathcal{F}[f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})]

satisfies Eq. (
39
) in the main text. We let
f_{\mathrm{aux}}
be as defined in Eq. (
40
) in the main text. We have

\displaystyle\quad\|f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}

(67)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f_{\ast}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}+\left\|K_{\gamma_{x}}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}+n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}3n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

(68)

In the above chain of derivations,
(a)
holds by applying
D.7
to the first term and applying
D.1
to the second term,
(b)
holds by
\|f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\leq\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\leq 1
by Plancherel’s Theorem and
2.3
.
Notice that,

\displaystyle(\ast):=\left\|\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-f_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}\leq\left\|\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-f_{\mathrm{aux}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}+\left\|f_{\ast}-f_{\mathrm{aux}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}.

(69)

By definition of
\hat{f}_{\lambda}
in Eq. (
7
) in the main text, it satisfies

\displaystyle\lambda\|\hat{f}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\left\langle f,\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(o_{i})\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}.

(70)

Notice that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}((Tf_{\ast})(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})+\upsilon_{i})^{2}\lesssim 2+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\upsilon_{i}^{2},

where the inequality follows from

\displaystyle\|Tf_{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}\leq\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}\leq\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\lesssim 1,

by
2.3
. By
4.5
,
\upsilon_{1},\ldots,\upsilon_{n}
are
n
i.i.d. mean zero
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian random variables so
\upsilon_{1}^{2},\ldots,\upsilon_{n}^{2}
are
n
i.i.d.
\sigma^{2}
-sub-exponential random variables
[
Vershynin 2018
, Lemma 2.7.6]
with mean
\E[\upsilon_{1}^{2}]<\infty
. By Exercise 2.7.10 (Centering) of
Vershynin 2018
we know that
\upsilon_{1}^{2}-\E[\upsilon_{1}^{2}],\ldots,\upsilon_{n}^{2}-\E[\upsilon_{n}^{2}]
are
n
i.i.d.
C\sigma^{2}
-sub-exponential random variables for some universal constant
C
. By
Vershynin 2018
, we have

\displaystyle(\forall t\geq 0),\;P\left(\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\upsilon_{i}^{2}\right|\geq n\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\upsilon_{i}^{2}\right]\right)\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-c\min\left(\frac{n}{C^{2}\sigma^{4}},\frac{n}{C\sigma^{2}}\right)\right),

where
c
is a universal constant. For a fixed
\tau\geq 1
, for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, the above right hand side
\leq 2\exp(-\tau)
. Thus for a fixed
\tau\geq 1
, with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2e^{-\tau}
, for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}\lesssim 2+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\upsilon_{i}^{2}\leq 4+2\mathbb{E}\left[\upsilon_{i}^{2}\right].

Under the same high probability event, from Eq. (
70
), using
\lambda=\frac{1}{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\hat{f}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\lesssim\sqrt{4+2\mathbb{E}\left[\upsilon_{i}^{2}\right]}\sqrt{n}.

(71)

Also, we have

\displaystyle\|f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\leq 2\sqrt{n}

(72)

proved above in Eq. (
67
). Continuing from Eq. (
69
), we apply
D.9
to
\|[\hat{f}_{\lambda}]-f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}
, where we notice that
\hat{f}_{\lambda},f_{\mathrm{aux}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
, and we use Eq. (
71
) and Eq. (
72
) to verify the assumption of that lemma. We find, with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2e^{-\tau}
,

\displaystyle(\ast)\leq\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\left\|T\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-Tf_{\mathrm{aux}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}+\left\|f_{\ast}-f_{\mathrm{aux}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}.

From Eq. (
84
),
\|f_{\ast}-f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}
can be upper bounded (up to a constant) by the second last term above and hence subsumed.
Through a triangular inequality, we have with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2e^{-\tau}
,

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\left(\left\|T\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-Tf_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+\left\|Tf_{\ast}-Tf_{\mathrm{aux}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

(73)

We prove in Eq. (
83
) in
Section

D.2
that
\left\|Tf_{\ast}-Tf_{\mathrm{aux}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\leq n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}
, so we continue from above to obtain, with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2e^{-\tau}
,

\displaystyle(\ast)\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\left\|T\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-Tf_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}-\eta_{0}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}.

(74)

Note the last term of Eq. (
73
) is subsumed by the second term above since
\eta_{0}\geq\eta_{1}
.
Recall the definition of
\bar{f}_{\lambda}
in Eq. (
23
) in the main text. Then, we have, through a triangular inequality, with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2e^{-\tau}
, the following holds

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\left(\underbrace{\left\|T\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-T\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}}_{\text{Projected Stage I Error}}+\underbrace{\left\|T\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]-Tf_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}}_{\text{Projected Stage II Error}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}-\eta_{0}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}.

The projected stage I error can be upper bounded by
D.1
as
n>A_{\lambda,\tau}
is satisfied for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
proved in Eq. (
89
).

\displaystyle\text{Projected Stage I Error}=\left\|T\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-T\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\leq\tau n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}

holds with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-34e^{-\tau}
.
The projected stage II error can be upper bounded by
D.2
.

\displaystyle\text{Projected Stage II Error}=\left\|T\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]-Tf_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\leq\tau n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\cdot(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2}}

holds with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
. Combine the above two upper bounds, and we have

\displaystyle(\ast)=\left\|\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]-f_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}\leq\tau n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}-\eta_{0}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1+d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}

(75)

holds with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
1-40e^{-\tau}
. So the proof is concluded.
∎

Proposition D.1

(Projected stage-I error)
.

Suppose that the assumptions of
D.4
hold. Suppose that
n>A_{\lambda,\tau}
with
A_{\lambda,\tau}
defined in Eq. (
87
). Suppose Assumptions
2.2
hold. Suppose that
\tilde{n}\geq 1
satisfies Eq. (
21
). Let
\lambda\asymp n^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Then, with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-34e^{-\tau}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|T\left(\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]-\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\lesssim\tau n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Proof.

From
D.3
, with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-28e^{-\tau}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|T\left(\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]-\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\underbrace{\left(\frac{\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\left\|F_{*}-[\hat{F}_{\xi}]\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\right)}\right)}_{({\ddagger})}\left(1+\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{XO,\gamma_{x},\gamma_{0}}}\right).

By
D.6
, we have with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2e^{-\tau}

\displaystyle\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\lesssim\tau n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Under the probabilistic event that
D.3
holds, the bounds in
D.4
also hold, so we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}\right]-F_{*}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}\leq J\tau\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{{\dagger}}}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}},\quad\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq J\tau\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{{\dagger}}-1}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}},

hold for any
\zeta>0
.
J
is some constant independent of
\tilde{n},n
.
Thus under this probabilistic event, a sufficient condition so that
({\ddagger})\lesssim\frac{1}{n}
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{n}\geq(J\tau n)^{2\frac{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}{m^{{\dagger}}}}\vee(J^{2}\tau^{2}n)^{\frac{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}{m^{{\dagger}}-1}}.

This is satisfied since
\tilde{n}
satisfies Eq. (
21
).
Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}\right]-F_{*}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}\leq n^{-1},\quad\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq n^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

(76)

By the union bound, we have that with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-30e^{-\tau}
, the following bound holds

\displaystyle\left\|T\left(\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]-\left[\hat{f}_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\lesssim\tau n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Hence the proof is concluded.
∎

Proposition D.2

(Projected stage-II error)
.

Suppose Assumptions
4.3
,
4.2
,
4.1
,
2.3
,
2.2
hold. Let
\lambda\asymp n^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Then, with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
, for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|T\left(f_{\ast}-\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\leq 2C\tau(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2}}n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}

for some constant
C>0
independent of
n
.

Proof.

The proposition is proved through the following triangular inequality

\displaystyle\left\|T\left(f_{\ast}-\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|T\left(f_{\ast}-\left[f_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+\left\|T\left(\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]-[f_{\lambda}]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2}}.

The second last inequality holds by using
D.7
for the first term and
D.8
for the second term, which holds with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
.
∎

D.1
Projected Stage I Error

With the introduction of
\mathcal{H}_{FO}
and the partial isometry
V:\mathcal{H}_{XO}\to\mathcal{H}_{FO}
in
Appendix

C
, we define

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle:=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\left\langle h,V\Big(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\Big)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\right)^{2}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}^{2}

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(y_{i}-\left\langle h,V\Big(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\Big)\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\right)^{2}+\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}^{2}.

As shown in
Appendix

C
, we have
\hat{h}_{\lambda}=V\hat{f}_{\lambda}
and
\bar{h}_{\lambda}=V\bar{f}_{\lambda}
, where
V:\mathcal{H}_{XO}\to\mathcal{H}_{FO}
is the metric surjection map introduced in
Appendix

C
.
\hat{f}_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
is defined in Eq. (
7
) and
\bar{f}_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
is defined in Eq. (
23
) in the main text.

We further define

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}:\mathcal{H}_{FO}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}&=\left[V\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{o}_{1})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{1})\right),\dots,V\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{n},\mathbf{o}_{n})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{n})\right)\right]^{\ast}\\
\bm{\Phi}_{FO}:\mathcal{H}_{FO}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}&=\left[V\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{o}_{1})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{1})\right),\dots,V\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{n},\mathbf{o}_{n})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{n})\right)\right]^{\ast}\\
\mathbf{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}&,\quad\mathbf{Y}=[y_{1},\ldots,y_{n}]^{\top},\end{aligned}

(77)

and the following operators on
\mathcal{H}_{FO}

\displaystyle C_{\hat{F}O}:=\E\left[V\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(Z,O)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(O)\right)\otimes V\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(Z,O)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(O)\right)\right],

\displaystyle\quad\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}:=\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}

\displaystyle C_{FO}:=\E\left[V\left(F_{\ast}(Z,O)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(O)\right)\otimes V\left(F_{\ast}(Z,O)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(O)\right)\right],

\displaystyle\quad\hat{C}_{FO}:=\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}.

Hence, we have closed form expression for

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{n}\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y},\quad\bar{h}_{\lambda}=\frac{1}{n}\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}.

(78)

Proposition D.3
.

Fix
\tau\geq 1
. Suppose that the assumptions of
D.4
hold. Suppose that
n>A_{\lambda,\tau}
with
A_{\lambda,\tau}
defined in Eq. (
87
), and
\tilde{n}\geq 1
satisfies Eq. (
21
) in the main text. We have with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-28e^{-\tau}
, the following inequality holds

\displaystyle\left\|T[\hat{f}_{\lambda}]-T[\bar{f}_{\lambda}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau\lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\frac{\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\left\|\left[F_{*}-\hat{F}_{\xi}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\right)}\right)\left(1+\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\right).

Under the same probabilistic event, the bounds in
D.4
also hold.

Proof.

We find

\displaystyle\left\|T[\hat{f}_{\lambda}]-T[\bar{f}_{\lambda}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}=\left\|[\hat{h}_{\lambda}]-[\bar{h}_{\lambda}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\leq\left\|C_{FO}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\hat{h}_{\lambda}-\bar{h}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}.

The last step follows from Lemma 12 of
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
. We can proceed by plugging in the closed form expressions from Eq. (
78
) to have

\displaystyle\left\|C_{FO}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\hat{h}_{\lambda}-\bar{h}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|C_{FO}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}-\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|C_{FO}^{\frac{1}{2}}(C_{FO}+\lambda)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\cdot\left\|(C_{FO}+\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}-\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|(C_{FO}+\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}-\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle S_{-1}+S_{0},

where we define

\displaystyle S_{-1}

\displaystyle:=\left\|(C_{FO}+\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\left(\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}-\bm{\Phi}_{FO}\right)^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}

\displaystyle S_{0}

\displaystyle=\left\|(C_{FO}+\lambda)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\left(\hat{C}_{FO}-\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}\right)\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\mathbf{Y}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}

We bound
S_{-1}
with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-high probability by
D.3
and
S_{0}
in
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-high probability by
D.2
. We thus have, with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-28e^{-\tau}
, the following bound

\displaystyle\quad\left\|C_{FO}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\hat{h}_{\lambda}-\bar{h}_{\lambda}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}

\displaystyle\leq c\tau\sqrt{n}\left(\frac{\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{\ast}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\right)}\right)\left(\|\bar{h}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}+1\right).

\|\bar{h}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}=\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}
since
V
is a metric surjection.
∎

Proposition D.4

(CME rate)
.

Suppose Assumption
2.2
in the main text holds. Suppose
k_{\mathcal{O}}:\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{R}
and
k_{\mathcal{Z}}:\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R}
are Matérn reproducing kernels whose RKHSs
\mathcal{H}_{O}
and
\mathcal{H}_{Z}
are norm equivalent to
W_{2}^{t_{o}}(\mathcal{O})
and
W_{2}^{t_{z}}(\mathcal{Z})
with
m_{o}>t_{o}>d_{o}/2
,
m_{z}>t_{z}>d_{z}/2
and
\frac{m_{z}}{t_{z}}\wedge\frac{m_{o}}{t_{o}}\leq 2
. Define
m^{{\dagger}}=(m_{z}t_{z}^{-1})\wedge(m_{o}t_{o}^{-1})
and
d^{{\dagger}}=(d_{z}t_{z}^{-1})\vee(d_{o}t_{o}^{-1})
.
For
\zeta>0
arbitrarily small, we take
\xi=\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}}
, then with
P^{\tilde{n}}
-probability at least
1-4e^{-\tau}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}\leq J\tau\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{{\dagger}}}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}},\quad\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq J\tau\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{{\dagger}}-1}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}}.

J
is a constant independent of
\tilde{n}
.

Proof.

We are going to apply
Li et al. 2024a
to obtain the desired result. To this end, we need to verify the assumptions (EVD) and (SRC) made in
Li et al. 2024a
. Note that we let the assumption (EMB) of
Li et al. 2024a
be trivially verified with
\alpha=1
, as we prove in
D.5
that
F_{\ast}\in[\mathcal{G}]^{\beta}
with
\beta>1
, therefore
\beta+p>1+p>1=\alpha
falls in Case 2 of Theorem 3 of
Li et al. 2024a
.

Verification of (EVD)

Let
\phi_{Z}
(resp.
\phi_{O}
) denote the feature map of
\mathcal{H}_{Z}
(resp.
\mathcal{H}_{O}
). Define
C_{ZO}:\mathcal{H}_{Z}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{O}\to\mathcal{H}_{Z}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{O}
as the covariance operator.

\displaystyle C_{ZO}=\iint_{\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}}(\phi_{Z}(\mathbf{z})\otimes\phi_{O}(\mathbf{o}))\otimes(\phi_{Z}(\mathbf{z})\otimes\phi_{O}(\mathbf{o}))p_{ZO}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}.

We also define another two auxiliary covariance operators
\bar{C}_{Z}:\mathcal{H}_{Z}\to\mathcal{H}_{Z}
and
\bar{C}_{O}:\mathcal{H}_{O}\to\mathcal{H}_{O}
.

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{Z}=\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\phi_{Z}(\mathbf{z})\otimes\phi_{Z}(\mathbf{z})p_{Z}(\mathbf{z})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z},\quad\bar{C}_{O}=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\phi_{O}(\mathbf{o})\otimes\phi_{O}(\mathbf{o})p_{O}(\mathbf{o})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}.

Since
k_{Z}
and
k_{O}
are bounded,
C_{ZO},\bar{C}_{Z},\bar{C}_{O}
are all self-adjoint compact operators.
From
Edmunds and Triebel 1996
and
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
[Section 4], we have
\lambda_{i}(\bar{C}_{Z})\asymp i^{-2t_{z}/d_{z}}
and
\lambda_{i}(\bar{C}_{O})\asymp i^{-2t_{o}/d_{o}}
.
We know from Assumption
2.2
that
P_{ZO}
is equivalent to the Lebesgue measure, so by Jensen’s inequality, we have
C_{ZO}\leq\bar{C}_{Z}\otimes\bar{C}_{O}
, where
\otimes
here denotes an operator tensor product. Hence, from Lemma 17 of
Meunier et al. 2024a
we have
\lambda_{i}(C_{ZO})\leq\lambda_{i}(\bar{C}_{Z}\otimes\bar{C}_{O})
. Finally, from
D.4
we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(C_{ZO})\leq\lambda_{i}(\bar{C}_{Z}\otimes\bar{C}_{O})\lesssim i^{-2t_{z}/d_{z}\wedge 2t_{o}/d_{o}+\zeta}

for any
\zeta>0
.
Therefore, we have proved that (EVD) hold with
1/p=2t_{z}/d_{z}\wedge 2t_{o}/d_{o}-\zeta
.
Hence,
p=d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta
for any
\zeta>0
.

Verification of (SRC)

Let
\beta=m^{{\dagger}}
. By the definition of vector-valued interpolation space in
Li et al. 2024a
[Definition 2] and the Assumption that
P_{ZO}
is equivalent to the Lebesgue measure, we have that

\displaystyle[\mathcal{G}]^{\beta}

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\otimes[L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}),\mathcal{H}_{Z}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{O}]_{\beta,2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\cong}}\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\otimes[L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}),W^{t_{z}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z})\otimes W^{t_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{O})]_{\beta,2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\cong}}\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\otimes W^{t_{z}\beta}_{2}(\mathcal{Z})\otimes W^{t_{o}\beta}_{2}(\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ast)}}{{\supseteq}}\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\otimes W^{m_{z}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z})\otimes W^{m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\cong}}\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\otimes MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathbb{R})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\cong}}MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}).

where in
(a)
we use Corollary 10.13 and Theorem 10.46 of
Wendland 2004
, in
(b)
we use
C.7
, in
(c)
we use proposition 3.1 of
Sickel and Ullrich 2009
, which shows
MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})\cong W^{m_{z}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z})\otimes W^{m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{O})
, and in
(d)
we use
D.1
. Furthermore, the inclusion in
(\ast)
is a continuous embedding. We show in
D.5
that under
2.2
,
\|F_{\ast}\|_{MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}
is bounded by a constant. Hence we deduce from the above embedding that

\displaystyle F_{\ast}\in[\mathcal{G}]^{\beta},\quad\beta=m^{{\dagger}}=\frac{m_{z}}{t_{z}}\wedge\frac{m_{o}}{t_{o}}.

(79)

Hence
\|F_{\ast}\|_{[\mathcal{G}]^{\beta}}\leq C
for some constant
C>0
. Hence
F_{\ast}
satisfies (SRC) in
Li et al. 2024a
with
\beta=m^{{\dagger}}
.

Now we use Case 2 in Theorem 3 of
Li et al. 2024a
to obtain the following result: if we take
\xi=\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}}
, then for any
0\leq\gamma<m^{{\dagger}}
, the following bound on the
\gamma
-norm

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right]\right\|_{\gamma}^{2}\leq\tau^{2}\tilde{n}^{-\frac{m^{{\dagger}}-\gamma}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}}.

holds with
P^{\tilde{n}}
-probability at least
1-4e^{-\tau}
.
Therefore, noting that
m^{{\dagger}}>1
, by taking
\gamma=0
and
\gamma=1
, we obtain with
P^{\tilde{n}}
-probability at least
1-4e^{-\tau}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}\leq\tau\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{{\dagger}}}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}},\quad\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{*}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\tau\tilde{n}^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{m^{{\dagger}}-1}{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}}.

∎

Proposition D.5
.

Suppose that Assumption
2.2
in the main text is satisfied. Then,

\displaystyle\|F_{\ast}\|_{MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}\leq\sqrt{\binom{m_{z}+d_{z}-1}{d_{z}-1}}\sqrt{\binom{m_{o}+d_{o}-1}{d_{o}-1}}\rho.

Proof.

Notice that, by definition of
F_{\ast}
,

\displaystyle\|F_{\ast}\|_{MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{\mathcal{X}}\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})p(\mathbf{x}|\cdot,\cdot)\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\right\|_{MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left\|\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})p(\mathbf{x}|\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}.

In the last inequality above, we use
Hytönen et al. 2016
[Proposition 1.2.11].
Next, notice that

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})p(\mathbf{x}\mid\cdot,\cdot)\right\|_{MW^{m_{z},m_{o}}_{2}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}|\leq m_{z}}\sum_{|\bm{\beta}|\leq m_{o}}\int_{\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}}\left\|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}\left(\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}|\leq m_{z}}\sum_{|\bm{\beta}|\leq m_{o}}\int_{\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}}\left\|\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}|\leq m_{z}}\sum_{|\bm{\beta}|\leq m_{o}}\int_{\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}}\left|\partial^{\bm{\alpha}}_{\mathbf{z}}\partial^{\bm{\beta}}_{\mathbf{o}}p(\mathbf{x}|\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{z}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle\leq\binom{m_{z}+d_{z}-1}{d_{z}-1}\binom{m_{o}+d_{o}-1}{d_{o}-1}\rho^{2}.

In the last step,
\binom{m_{z}+d_{z}-1}{d_{z}-1}
shows up as the evaluation of
\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}|\leq m_{z}}1
.
∎

D.1.1
RKHS norm of
\bar{f}_{\lambda}

We now provide a refined control of the RKHS norm of
\bar{f}_{\lambda}
by invoking an oracle inequality for the RKHS
\mathcal{H}_{FO}
. We remind the reader that
V\bar{f}_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{H}_{FO}
, i.e. the image of
\bar{f}_{\lambda}
under the metric surjection
V
, is the solution to a KRR problem with respect to the RKHS
\mathcal{H}_{FO}
(see Eq. (
59
)). In
Section

C.1
we characterized the rate of decay of the entropy numbers of
\mathcal{H}_{FO}
. The only remaining technical hurdle is that the noise
\upsilon=Y-(Tf_{\ast})(Z,O)
is unbounded; however, under
4.5
it is
\sigma
-subgaussian. Concretely, we introduce a logarithmically growing sequence of clipping values
M_{n}
, which facilitates a key step in the derivation of the oracle inequality and ensures that the conclusions of
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
are applicable.

Firstly, by
Vershynin 2018
,
P(|\upsilon|\geq t)\leq 2\exp(-\frac{ct^{2}}{\sigma^{2}})
for some universal constant
c
.
Define
\upsilon_{i}=y_{i}-(Tf_{\ast})(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})
for stage II samples
\{\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i},y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
.
Hence

\displaystyle P^{n}\left(\{\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i},y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}\in(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}\times Y)^{n}:\max_{i}|\upsilon_{i}|\leq t\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-\sum_{i=1}^{n}P(|\upsilon_{i}|\geq t)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 1-2\exp\left(\ln n-\frac{ct^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).

Hence, with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2\exp(-\hat{\rho})
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq n}|\upsilon_{i}|\leq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\ln n+\hat{\rho}}{c}}.

By
2.3
, we have
\|Tf_{\ast}\|_{\infty}\leq\|f_{\ast}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
. For
n\geq 1
, we define

\displaystyle M_{n}=1+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\ln n+\hat{\rho}}{c}}.

Hence
y_{i}\in[-M_{n},M_{n}]
for all
1\leq i\leq n
with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2\exp(-\hat{\rho})
.

Secondly, we verify the assumptions of
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
. By
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
, the
supremum bound
is satisfied for
B=4M_{n}^{2}
and the
variance bound
is satisfied for
V=16M_{n}^{2}
and
\nu=1
.
Define
D_{ZO}=\{\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
L^{2}(D_{ZO})
as the
L^{2}
space with respect to the empirical data measure
D_{ZO}
.
We are about to show that
(\forall n\geq 1)(\exists p\in(0,1))(\exists a\geq B=4M_{n}^{2})
such that

\displaystyle(\forall i\geq 1)\;\mathbb{E}_{D_{ZO}\sim P_{ZO}^{n}}e_{i}(\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{H}_{FO}\to L^{2}(D_{ZO}))\leq ai^{-\frac{1}{2p}}.

(80)

To this end, we invoke
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
,
C.1
and
C.2
. We conclude that, there exists a constant
c_{p}>0
only depending on
p
, such that
(\forall i\geq 1)(\forall n\geq 1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{D_{ZO}\sim P^{n}_{ZO}}e_{i}(\mathrm{id}:\mathcal{H}_{FO}\hookrightarrow L^{2}(D_{ZO}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}(3C)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\left(\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2ep}\right)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2p}}\left(\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2p}}(\min\{i,n\})^{\frac{1}{2p}}i^{-\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}(3C)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\left(\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2ep}\right)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2p}}\left(\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2p}}i^{-\frac{1}{2p}}.

We define a new constant
\tilde{c}_{p}:=c_{p}(3C)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\left(\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2ep}\right)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}{2p}}
. Since for all
n\geq 1
,
\left(\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2p}}\geq 1
, we set

\displaystyle a=\max\{4M_{n}^{2},\tilde{c}_{p}\}\left(\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{-\frac{1}{2p}}

in Eq. (
80
), which satisfies
a\geq B=4M_{n}^{2}
for all
n\geq 1
.

We thus apply
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
restricted to the probabilistic event where
y_{i}\in[-2M_{n},2M_{n}]
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.
We find, with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-5\exp(-\hat{\rho})
,

\displaystyle\lambda\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{XO}}\leq 9\left(\lambda\|f_{0}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{XO}}+\|T(f_{0}-f_{\ast})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\right)

\displaystyle+K\left(\frac{\max\{4M_{n}^{2},\tilde{c}_{p}\}^{2p}\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}}{\lambda^{p}n}\right)+\frac{216M_{n}^{2}\hat{\rho}}{n}+\frac{15B_{0}\hat{\rho}}{n}

where
K\geq 1
is a constant depending on
p,M_{n}
,
f_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
.
B_{0}\geq B
is a constant that satisfies

\displaystyle\|L\circ(Tf_{0})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o},y)\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}\times[-2M_{n},2M_{n}]}(y-(Tf_{0})(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}))^{2}\leq B_{0},

By checking the dependence of
K
on
p,M_{n}
in the proof of
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
, we find that
K\leq c(p)M_{n}^{2}
for some constant
c(p)
depending only on
p
.
We choose
f_{0}=f_{\lambda}
, where
f_{\lambda}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
is defined in Eq. (
57
). We have, since
Y=[-2M_{n},2M_{n}]
,

\displaystyle\|L\circ(Tf_{\lambda})\|_{\infty}=

\displaystyle\sup_{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o},y)\in\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}\times[-2M_{n},2M_{n}]}(y-(Tf_{\lambda})(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}))^{2}\leq(2M_{n}+\|Tf_{\lambda}\|_{\infty})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2M_{n}+\|f_{\lambda\|_{\infty}})^{2}\leq(2M_{n}+\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}})^{2}.

We choose thus

\displaystyle B_{0}=(2M_{n}+\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}})^{2}\geq 4M_{n}^{2}=B.

Hence we deduce from the above high probability inequality that

\displaystyle\lambda\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{XO}}\leq C\left(\lambda\|f_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{XO}}+\|T(f_{\lambda}-f_{\ast})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\right)

\displaystyle+C\left(M_{n}^{2+2p}\left(\frac{\max\{4,\tilde{c}_{p}\}^{2p}\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}}{\lambda^{p}n}\right)+\frac{(M_{n}+\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{XO}})^{2}\hat{\rho}}{n}\right).

for some constant
C
that is independent of
\lambda,M_{n},n,\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o},B,V,\hat{\rho}
. We now state the main Proposition in this subsection.

Proposition D.6
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.2
,
2.3
,
4.1
,
4.2
and
4.3
in the main text hold.
Let
\lambda=n^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Fix an arbitrary
p>0
. Then with probability
\geq 1-5e^{-\tau}
, for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, the following bound holds

\displaystyle\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\lesssim\tau n^{\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}+p}.

Proof.

Fix
p>0
. We combine the results of
D.7
with the inequality immediately preceding this proposition to find, for
\hat{\rho}\geq 1
, there exists a constant
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}
independent of
\lambda,M_{n},n,\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o},B,V,\hat{\rho}
such that , with probability
\geq 1-5e^{-\hat{\rho}}
the following bound holds

\displaystyle\quad\lambda\|\bar{f}_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{XO}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}C_{1}\hat{\rho}\left(\lambda\|f_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{XO}}+\|T(f_{\lambda}-f_{\ast})\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+M_{n}^{2+2p}\left(\frac{\max\{4,\tilde{c}_{p}\}^{2p}\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}}{\lambda^{p}n}\right)+\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}C_{2}\hat{\rho}\left(n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}+M_{n}^{2+2p}\left(\frac{\max\{4,\tilde{c}_{p}\}^{2p}\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}}{\lambda^{p}n}\right)+\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}C_{2}\hat{\rho}\left(n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\left(1+M_{n}^{2+2p}\frac{\max\{4,\tilde{c}_{p}\}^{2p}}{\lambda^{p}}\right)+\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}C_{3}\hat{\rho}n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}n^{2p}.

In the above derivations,
(a)
holds by
\lambda=n^{-1}
,
(b)
holds by the conclusion of
D.7
,
(c)
holds by the choice of
\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}
as functions of
n
,
(d)
holds
for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, since
p
is fixed and
n^{p}>M_{n}^{2+2p}=\left(1+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\ln n+\hat{\rho}}{c}}\right)^{2+2p}
for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, and
n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\geq\frac{M_{n}^{2}}{n}
for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
. In particular, the constant
C_{3}
depends on
p
.
∎

D.2
Projected approximation error in Stage II

Proposition D.7
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.2
,
2.3
,
4.1
,
4.2
and
4.3
in the main text hold.
Let
\lambda=n^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\lambda\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}+\left\|T\left(f_{\ast}-\left[f_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}\leq Cn^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},

for some constant
C>0
independent of
n
.

Proof.

Recall
f_{\mathrm{aux}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
defined in Eq. (
40
). We write

\displaystyle f_{\mathrm{aux}}=f_{\ast}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}-(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}+(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast K_{\gamma_{x}}.

By definition of
f_{\lambda}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\lambda\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}+\left\|T\left(f_{\ast}-\left[f_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\inf_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\lambda\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}+\left\|Tf_{\ast}-T[f]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\|f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}+\left\|Tf_{\ast}-T[f_{\mathrm{aux}}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\|f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}+\left\|Tf_{\ast}-T\left(f_{\ast}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\left\|T\left((f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}-(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast K_{\gamma_{x}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}.

(81)

For the first term, we know from Eq. (
67
) and
2.3
in the main text that

\displaystyle\lambda\|f_{\mathrm{aux}}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}\leq n^{-1}\cdot 2n^{\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\leq 2n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

For the second term, we have

\displaystyle\left\|Tf_{\ast}-T\left(f_{\ast}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f_{\ast}-f_{\ast}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|f_{\ast}-f_{\ast}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim|f_{\ast}|^{2}_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\cdot\max\{0,\gamma_{o}^{2s_{o}}\}

\displaystyle=|f_{\ast}|^{2}_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

The first inequality above holds because
T
is a bounded operator;
the second inequality holds by Assumption
2.2
that
P_{XO}
admits a bounded density.
The second last inequality above holds by using
D.5
for
\bm{\gamma}=(\underbrace{0,\dots,0}_{d_{x}},\underbrace{\gamma_{o},\dots,\gamma_{o}}_{d_{o}})
.

For the third term, we note that
(\forall\mathbf{o}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\left((f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}-(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast K_{\gamma_{x}}\right)(\cdot,\mathbf{o})]

is supported on the complement of
\{\mathbf{x}:\|\mathbf{x}\|\leq\gamma_{x}^{-1}\}
(see Eq. (
38
)). Thus it follows from
4.3
that

\displaystyle\quad\left\|T\left((f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}-(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast K_{\gamma_{x}}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{x}^{2d_{x}\eta_{1}}\left\|(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}-(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast K_{\gamma_{x}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\lesssim}}\gamma_{x}^{2d_{x}\eta_{1}+2s_{x}}\cdot\left|(f_{\ast}-f_{\ast,\mathrm{low}})\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\right|^{2}_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\gamma_{x}^{2d_{x}\eta_{1}+2s_{x}}\cdot\left|f_{\ast}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\right|^{2}_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\gamma_{x}^{2d_{x}\eta_{1}+2s_{x}}\cdot\left|f_{\ast}\right|^{2}_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\|K_{\gamma_{o}}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{\lesssim}}n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

In the above derivations,
(i)
follows by using
D.5
for
\bm{\gamma}=(\underbrace{\gamma_{x},\dots,\gamma_{x}}_{d_{x}},\underbrace{0,\dots,0}_{d_{o}})
and the Assumption that
P_{XO}
admits a bounded density,
(ii)
follows from the proof of
E.3
,
(iii)
follows from
E.2
, and
(iv)
follows by the fact that
\|K_{\gamma_{o}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R})}=1

[
Giné and Nickl 2021
, Section 4.1.2]
and
2.3
in the main text.

Combine the upper bound on the three terms in Eq. (
81
) and we obtain

\displaystyle\lambda\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}+\left\|T\left(f_{\ast}-\left[f_{\lambda}\right]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}\leq n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

(82)

The proof concludes here.
In addition to that, from Eq. (
81
), we also have

\displaystyle\left\|Tf_{\ast}-T[f_{\mathrm{aux}}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\leq Cn^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

(83)

Also, if we follow the same derivations as above, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\ast}-[f_{\mathrm{aux}}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}\leq Cn^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

(84)

∎

D.3
Projected estimation error in Stage II

Proposition D.8
.

Suppose Assumptions
2.2
,
2.3
,
4.1
,
4.2
and
4.3
in the main text hold.
Let
\lambda\asymp n^{-1}
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

With
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
, for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|T\left(\left[\bar{f}_{\lambda}\right]-[f_{\lambda}]]\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}\leq C\tau^{2}n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1},

for some constant
C
independent of
n
.

Proof.

Recall the definition of
\bar{h}_{\lambda}
defined in Eq. (
60
) and
h_{\lambda}
defined in Eq. (
59
).

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\lambda\|h\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-y_{i})^{2}.

\displaystyle h_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\lambda\|h\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}+\|h_{\ast}-h\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}.

Recall that we have proved that
\|T([\bar{f}_{\lambda}]-[f_{\lambda}])\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}=\|[\bar{h}_{\lambda}]-[h_{\lambda}]\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}
in Eq. (
61
), so the proof of
D.8
is translated to the estimation error of a standard kernel ridge regression with hypothesis space
\mathcal{H}_{FO}
and the target function
h_{\ast}:=Tf_{\ast}\in L^{2}(P_{ZO})
. Next, we are going to apply existing results, mainly Theorem 16 of
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
, to our setting.

From Eq. (
61
) and
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
[Lemma 12], we have

\displaystyle\left\|[\bar{h}_{\lambda}]-[h_{\lambda}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}\leq\left\|C_{FO}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\bar{h}_{\lambda}-h_{\lambda}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}=:(\ast)

(85)

Next, we will upper bound
(\ast)
using Theorem 16 from
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
. To apply this result, we must verify the underlying assumptions and control the auxiliary quantities specified in Theorem 16 from
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
.

First, we are going to verify the (MOM) condition.
By
4.5
, we know that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left|y-(Tf_{\ast})(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\right|^{m}p(y\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})dy

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[|\upsilon|^{m}\mid Z=\mathbf{z},O=\mathbf{o}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{2}C\sigma)^{m}(m/2)^{\frac{m}{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}(2C\sigma)^{m}m!

by
Vershynin 2018
, for some universal constant
C>0
. Hence the (MOM) condition of
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
is satisfied.

Next, we control the auxiliary quantities in
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
[Theorem 16]. Recall
m_{z},m_{o}
as defined in Eq. (
19
). By definition, they satisfy

\displaystyle\frac{d_{o}}{2m_{o}}<\frac{1+2\left(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}\right)}{1+2\left(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}\right)+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}\left(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}\right)}<1,\quad\frac{d_{z}}{2m_{z}}\leq\frac{d_{o}}{2m_{o}}<1.

Rearranging, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d_{o}}{2m_{o}}

\displaystyle<\left(2m_{o}\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}+1\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle\frac{d_{o}}{2m_{o}}

\displaystyle<\frac{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})+\frac{2m_{o}+d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}.

Hence there exists
\theta\in(0,1)
such that the following inequalities hold simultaneously

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{d_{o}}{2m_{o}\theta}<1,\quad\frac{d_{z}}{2m_{z}\theta}<1\\
\theta\leq\frac{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})+\frac{2m_{o}+d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}\\
\theta\left(2m_{o}\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}+1\right)<1\end{aligned}

(86)

Next, we define

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} g_{\lambda}&:=\log\left(2e\mathcal{N}_{FO}(\lambda)\frac{\|C_{FO}\|+\lambda}{\|C_{FO}\|}\right)\\
A_{\lambda,\tau}&:=8\left\|k^{\theta}_{FO}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\tau g_{\lambda}\lambda^{-\theta}\\
L_{\lambda}&:=\max\left\{L,\left\|h_{\ast}-[h_{\lambda}]\right\|_{L^{\infty}(P_{ZO})}\right\}\end{aligned}

(87)

Controlling
g_{\lambda}
and
A_{\lambda,\tau}
:

From
C.1
, we know that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{FO}(\lambda)\lesssim(\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}\left(\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{-1}=(\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}n^{\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

From
D.10
, we know that

\displaystyle\|C_{FO}\|\geq a_{f}^{-1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{4}\right)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Therefore, since
\lambda\asymp n^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle g_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\log\left(2e\mathcal{N}_{FO}(\lambda)\left(1+\lambda\|C_{FO}\|^{-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log\left((\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}n^{\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\left(1+n^{-1}\cdot n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(2(\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}n^{\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\right)

\displaystyle=(d_{x}+d_{o}+1)\log(\log n)+\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}\log(n)

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}\log(n).

The last step holds because
\log(n)
dominates a constant term and
\log(\log n)
for sufficiently large
n
. Since
\frac{d_{o}}{2m_{o}\theta}<1,\frac{d_{z}}{2m_{z}\theta}<1
, by
C.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|k_{FO}^{\theta}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\rho^{\theta}\gamma_{o}^{-\theta m_{o}}.

(88)

Therefore,

\displaystyle A_{\lambda,\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|k_{FO}^{\theta}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\tau\log(n)n^{\theta}\lesssim\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}\theta}\tau\log(n)n^{\theta}=\tau\log(n)n^{\theta\left(2m_{o}\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}+1\right)}.

(89)

Since
\theta(2m_{o}\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}+1)<1
from Eq. (
86
),
n>A_{\lambda,\tau}
holds for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
.

Controlling
L_{\lambda}
:

By Eq. (
82
), we have

\displaystyle\lambda\|f_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\leq n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f_{\ast}\|_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}^{2}.

Thus

\displaystyle\|f_{\lambda}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\leq n^{\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f_{\ast}\|_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}^{2}.

(90)

Thus we have

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{2C\sigma,\|T(f_{\ast}-[f_{\lambda}])\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{2C\sigma,\|f_{\ast}-[f_{\lambda}]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{2C\sigma,\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}+\|[f_{\lambda}]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\max\left\{2C\sigma,\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}+\|f_{\lambda}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\max\left\{2C\sigma,\|f_{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}+n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f_{\ast}\|_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\right\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}2n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f_{\ast}\|_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})},

(91)

where
(i)
holds by the reproducing property,
(ii)
holds by Eq. (
90
), and
(iii)
holds for sufficiently large
n
.

Bounding
(\ast)
:
Now we are ready to apply
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
[Theorem 16] to upper bound
(\ast)
.
By
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
[Theorem 16], with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
, for
n\geq A_{\lambda,\tau}
,

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle=\left\|C_{FO}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\bar{h}_{\lambda}-h_{\lambda}\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{576\tau^{2}}{n}\left(\sigma^{2}\mathcal{N}_{FO}(\lambda)+\left\|k^{\theta}_{FO}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\frac{\left\|h_{\ast}-[h_{\lambda}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}}{\lambda^{\theta}}+2\left\|k^{\theta}_{FO}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\frac{L_{\lambda}^{2}}{n\lambda^{\theta}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\frac{\tau^{2}}{n}\left((\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}n^{\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}+\left\|k^{\theta}_{FO}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}n^{\theta}\left(\left\|h_{\ast}-[h_{\lambda}]\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}^{2}+\frac{L_{\lambda}^{2}}{n}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\tau^{2}\left(n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}+\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}\theta}n^{\theta-1}\cdot n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\lesssim}}

\displaystyle\tau^{2}\left(n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}+n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\tau^{2}n^{-\frac{2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{d_{x}+d_{o}+1}.

We use Proposition
C.1
to upper bound
\mathcal{N}_{FO}(\lambda)
in
(a)
,
we use Eq. (
91
) to upper bound
L_{\lambda}
, Eq. (
88
) to upper bound
\|k^{\theta}_{FO}\|_{\infty}
and
D.7
to upper bound
\|h_{\ast}-[h_{\lambda}]\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}=\|Tf_{\ast}-T[f_{\lambda}]\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO}}
in
(b)
, and we use the following fact in
(c)
: by Eq. (
86
), we have

\displaystyle\theta\leq\frac{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})+\frac{2m_{o}+d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle n^{\frac{\frac{2m_{o}\theta}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}n^{\theta-1}\leq 1

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\gamma_{o}^{-2m_{o}\theta}n^{\theta-1}\leq 1.

The proof is concluded.
∎

D.4
Auxiliary Lemmas for
Appendix

D

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\Omega_{1},\Omega_{2}
be two open sets in
\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}}
respectively. Let
\mathcal{H}
be a Hilbert space. Then we have

\displaystyle MW^{m,p}(\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H})\cong W^{m}(\Omega_{1},W^{p}(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H}))\cong W^{m}(\Omega_{1})\otimes W^{p}(\Omega_{2})\otimes\mathcal{H}

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
Aubin 2011
[Theorem 12.7.2]. Let
f\in W^{m}(\Omega_{1},W^{p}(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H}))
. Then
f\in L^{2}(\Omega_{1},W^{p}(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H}))
such that
\partial_{1}^{\alpha}f\in L^{2}(\Omega_{1},W^{p}(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H}))
for
|\alpha|\leq m
. As explained in
Hytönen et al. 2016
[Definition 2.5.1], to say that
\partial_{1}^{\alpha}f\in L^{2}(\Omega_{1},W^{p}(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H}))
is equivalent to saying that for every
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Omega_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{1}}f(x,\cdot)\partial^{\alpha}_{1}\phi(x)\;\mathrm{d}x=(-1)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\Omega_{1}}(\partial^{\alpha}_{1}f)(x,\cdot)\phi(x)\;\mathrm{d}x

(92)

belongs to
W^{p}(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H})
. But this exactly says that
f\in MW^{m,p}(\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2},\mathcal{H})
. Thus the first isometric isomorphism is proved. The second isomorphism is proved by two successive applications of
Aubin 2011
, namely the fact that the Hilbert-space valued Sobolev space
W^{m}(\Omega,\mathcal{H})
is isometrically isomorphic to the Hilbertian tensor product
W^{m}(\Omega)\otimes\mathcal{H}
.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Fix
\tau\geq 1
. Suppose that the assumptions of
D.4
hold. Let
\lambda=\frac{1}{n}
. We assume that
n\geq 1
satisfies
n>A_{\lambda,\tau}
with
A_{\lambda,\tau}
defined in Eq. (
87
) and
\tilde{n}\geq 1
satisfies Eq. (
21
). Then, with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-10e^{-\tau}

\displaystyle S_{0}\leq c^{\prime}\tau\sqrt{n}\left(\frac{\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{\ast}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}\right]-F_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\right)}\right)\left\|\bar{h}_{\lambda}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}},

where
c^{\prime}
is a constant independent of
n,\tilde{n}
. Under the same probabilistic event, the bounds in
D.4
hold.

Proof.

We adapt the proof of
Meunier et al. 2024a
[Theorem 12].

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} S_{0}=&\left\|\left(C_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{1/2}\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}-\hat{C}_{FO}\right)\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\\
\leq&\lambda^{-1/2}\underbrace{\left\|\left(C_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{1/2}\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1/2}\right\|}_{(A)}\\
&\cdot\underbrace{\left\|\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{1/2}\left(\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}+\lambda\right)^{-1/2}\right\|}_{(B)}\cdot\underbrace{\left\|\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}-\hat{C}_{FO}\right\|}_{(C)}\cdot\left\|\bar{h}_{\lambda}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}\end{aligned}

Upper bound for term
(A)
. Notice that all assumptions from
Fischer and Steinwart 2020
have been checked already in the analysis of projected estimation error in
Section

D.3
.
Since
n\geq A_{\lambda,\tau}
with
A_{\lambda,\tau}
defined in Eq. (
87
), with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-2e^{-\tau}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(C_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1/2}\left(C_{FO}-\hat{C}_{FO}\right)\left(C_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1/2}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\left\|k^{\theta}_{FO}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\tau g_{\lambda}}{3n\lambda^{\theta}}+\sqrt{\frac{2\left\|k^{\theta}_{FO}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\tau g_{\lambda}}{n\lambda^{\theta}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}.

By
Rudi et al. 2015
, we have

\displaystyle(A)\leq\left(1-\left\|\left(C_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1/2}\left(C_{FO}-\hat{C}_{FO}\right)\left(C_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-1/2}\right\|\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq(1/3)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq 2.

Upper bound for term
(C)
. Define

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{\hat{F}O}:\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{o}_{1})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{1})\right),\dots,\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{n},\mathbf{o}_{n})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{n})\right)\right]^{\ast}

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{FO}:\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
