\displaystyle=\left[\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{o}_{1})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{1})\right),\dots,\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{n},\mathbf{o}_{n})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{1})\right)\right]^{\ast}.

An immediate consequence is that
V\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}=\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}
and
V\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{FO}^{\ast}=\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}
for
V
defined following
12
and
\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O},\bm{\Phi}_{FO}
defined in Eq. (
77
).
Hence, we have

\displaystyle(C)

\displaystyle=\left\|\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}-\hat{C}_{FO}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}^{\ast}\bm{\Phi}_{FO}-\frac{1}{n}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\bm{\Phi}_{\hat{F}O}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}V\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{FO}^{\ast}\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{FO}V^{\ast}-\frac{1}{n}V\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{\hat{F}O}V^{\ast}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{FO}^{\ast}\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{FO}-\frac{1}{n}\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{\hat{F}O}^{\ast}\tilde{\bm{\Phi}}_{\hat{F}O}\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\right\|.

The second last equality holds because
V:\mathcal{H}_{XO}\to\mathcal{H}_{FO}
is an isometry.
We start with the following decomposition

\displaystyle\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\left(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i}))

\displaystyle+(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i}))\otimes\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right).

Thus we have

\displaystyle(C)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\right\|

\displaystyle+2\left\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i}))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\right\|

\displaystyle+2\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\left(\left(\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\right)\otimes(F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i}))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}}^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})-F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}},

where in the last step we use
\|a\otimes b\|_{H_{1}\otimes H_{2}}=\|a\|_{H_{1}}\|b\|_{H_{2}}
for
a\in H_{1}
,
b\in H_{2}
for Hilbert spaces
H_{1},H_{2}

[
Gretton et al. 2005
, Eq. (3)]
,
\|\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}_{i})\|\leq 1
and
\|F_{\ast}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}}\leq 1
.
Note that the last line is exactly analyzed in the proof of
Meunier et al. 2024a
. Their analysis employs a Hoeffding’s concentration bounds with respect to Stage 2 samples
\mathcal{D}_{2}
, and
conditioned
on Stage 1 samples
\mathcal{D}_{1}
to show that, conditioned on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
,

\displaystyle(C)\leq J_{0}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\tau}{n}}\left\|F_{\ast}-\hat{F}_{\xi}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}+\left\|F_{*}-[\hat{F}_{\xi}]\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\right)}\right)

(93)

with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
, under the assumptions that

\displaystyle\left\|F_{\ast}-[\hat{F}_{\xi}]\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\right)}\vee\left\|F_{\ast}-\hat{F}_{\xi}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq 1.

(94)

We note that the independence of
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
is implicitly used in this step to ensure that
\mathcal{D}_{2}
remains i.i.d. after conditioning on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
.
J_{0}
is a constant independent of
n,\tilde{n}
.
Denote as
\mathfrak{D}
the
P^{\tilde{n}}
-probabilistic event that the bounds in
D.4
hold (which has
P^{\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
). Under this event
\mathfrak{D}
along with the fact that
\tilde{n}
satisfies Eq. (
21
), Eq. (
94
) is satisfied so Eq. (
93
) holds. Additionally, from Eq. (
93
), a sufficient condition for
(C)\leq\frac{1}{6n}
is given by
\tilde{n}\geq(6JJ_{0}\tau^{\frac{3}{2}}n)^{2\frac{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}{m^{{\dagger}}}}\vee(36J^{2}J_{0}^{2}\tau^{2}n)^{\frac{m^{{\dagger}}+d^{{\dagger}}/2+\zeta}{m^{{\dagger}}-1}}
, which is satisfied since
\tilde{n}
satisfies Eq. (
21
).

Upper bound for term
(B)
. By
Rudi et al. 2015
, we have
(B)\leq(1-t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
, where

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle:=\left\|\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\hat{C}_{FO}-\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}\right)\left(\hat{C}_{FO}+\lambda\right)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|\leq\lambda^{-1}\left\|\hat{C}_{FO}-\hat{C}_{\hat{F}O}\right\|=n\cdot(C)\leq\frac{1}{6},

from where we have
(B)\leq\frac{6}{5}
. Under the event
\mathfrak{D}
, the upper bounds
(A)\leq 2
,
(B)\leq 6/5
,
(C)\leq\frac{1}{6}
hold simultaneously with
P^{n}
-probability
\geq 1-6e^{-\tau}
. Since
\mathfrak{D}
holds with
P^{\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-4e^{-\tau}
, by independence of Stage 1 and Stage 2 samples (which is a consequence of the sample splitting strategy), we have that the above upper bounds hold simultaneously with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq\left(1-6e^{-\tau}\right)\left(1-4e^{-\tau}\right)\geq 1-10e^{-\tau}
.
∎

Lemma D.3
.

Fix
\tau\geq 1
. Suppose that the assumptions of
D.4
hold. Let
\lambda=\frac{1}{n}
. We assume that
n\geq 1
satisfies
n\geq A_{\lambda,\tau}
with
A_{\lambda,\tau}
defined in Eq. (
87
) and
\tilde{n}\geq 1
satisfies Eq. (
21
). Then, with
P^{n+\tilde{n}}
-probability
\geq 1-18e^{-\tau}

\displaystyle S_{-1}\leq c\tau\sqrt{n}\left(\frac{\left\|\hat{F}_{\xi}-F_{\ast}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}}{\sqrt{n}}+\left\|\left[\hat{F}_{\xi}\right]-F_{\ast}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O};\mathcal{H}_{X,\gamma_{x}}\right)}\right)\left\|\bar{h}_{\lambda}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{FO}}.

where
c
is a constant independent of
\tilde{n},n
. Under the same probabilistic event, the bounds in
D.4
hold.

Proof.

We omit the proof since it is similar to
Meunier et al. 2024a
, with adaptations similar to those in the proof of
D.2
.
∎

Lemma D.4
.

Suppose the eigenvalues of the operator
\Sigma_{1}
satisfy
\mu_{1,i}\asymp i^{-1/p_{1}}
and the eigenvalues of the operator
\Sigma_{2}
satisfy
\mu_{2,i}\asymp i^{-1/p_{2}}
. Then the eigenvalues of their tensor product
\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2}
satisfy, for any
\zeta>0
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{i}(\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2})\leq i^{-1/p_{1}\wedge 1/p_{2}+\zeta}.

Proof.

Suppose
\left(\mu_{1,i}\right)_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
(resp.
\left(\mu_{2,j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
) are the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{1}
(resp.
\Sigma_{2}
) with corresponding eigenfunctions
\left(q_{1,i}\right)_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
(resp.
\left(q_{2,j}\right)_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
).
By definition of operator tensor product, we have

\displaystyle\left(\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2}\right)(q_{1,i}\otimes q_{2,j})=\left(\Sigma_{1}q_{1,i}\right)\otimes\left(\Sigma_{2}q_{2,j}\right)=\mu_{1,i}\mu_{2,j}(q_{1,i}\otimes q_{2,j}).

Therefore, for any
i\in\mathbb{N}^{+},j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}
we obtain that
\mu_{1,i}\cdot\mu_{2,j}
is the eigenvalue of
\Sigma_{1}\otimes_{op}\Sigma_{2}
with corresponding eigenfunction
q_{1,i}\otimes q_{2,j}
.
Therefore the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{1}\otimes_{op}\Sigma_{2}
equal the tensor product of two sequences
(\mu_{1,i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
and
(\mu_{2,j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
.

Without loss of generality, we assume
p_{1}\leq p_{2}
so that
\mu_{1,i}\leq\mu_{2,i}
for
i
large enough.
Denote
(\sigma_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
as the tensor product of two sequences
(\mu_{2,i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
and
(\mu_{2,j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}^{+}}
rearranged in a non-increasing order. Hence, we have
\lambda_{n}(\Sigma_{1}\otimes\Sigma_{2})\leq\sigma_{n}
.
From
Krieg 2018
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}\leq n^{-1/p_{2}}(\log n)^{1/p_{2}}\leq n^{-1/p_{2}+\zeta},

for any
\zeta>0
.
It concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.5
.

Let
\mathcal{X}=[0,1]^{d}
. Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and
f\in B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\bm{s}=[s_{1},\ldots,s_{d}]^{\top}
. Recall
K_{\bm{\gamma}}
defined in Eq. (
66
) with
\bm{\gamma}=[\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{d}]^{\top}\in(0,1]^{d}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\left[K_{\bm{\gamma}}\ast f\right]-f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\leq C_{0}|f|_{B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}\cdot\max\{\gamma_{1}^{s_{1}},\ldots,\gamma_{d}^{s_{d}}\}

for some constant
C_{0}
that only depends on
d,\bm{s}
.

Proof.

Notice that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad K_{\bm{\gamma}}*f(\mathbf{x})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\frac{1}{j^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\right)\exp\left(-2\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{(x_{i}-t_{i})^{2}}{j^{2}\gamma_{i}^{2}}\right)f(\bm{t})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{t}\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\frac{1}{j^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-2\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{(x_{i}-t_{i})^{2}}{j^{2}\gamma_{i}^{2}}\right)f(\bm{t})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{t}\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\frac{1}{j^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-2\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{h_{i}^{2}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}\right)f(\mathbf{x}+j\bm{h})j^{d}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)f(\mathbf{x}+j\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}f(\mathbf{x}+j\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma})\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}.\end{aligned}

Next, since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}
= 1, we have that

\displaystyle\quad\left\|[K_{\bm{\gamma}}*f]-f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}f(\mathbf{x}+j\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma})\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{2r+1-j}f(\mathbf{x}+j\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma})\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(-1)^{r+1}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\Delta_{\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma}}^{r}f(\mathbf{x})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}.

Where the last step follows from the definition of modulus of smoothness in Eq. (
9
).
Then, from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{X}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left|\Delta_{\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma}}^{r}f(\mathbf{x})\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left|\Delta_{\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma}}^{r}f(\mathbf{x})\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left\|\Delta_{\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma}}^{r}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}.

(95)

Define
N:=\min\{\gamma_{1}^{-s_{1}},\ldots,\gamma_{d}^{-s_{d}}\}
.
Since
f\in B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})
,
we have

\displaystyle|f|_{B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}\geq|f|_{B_{2,\infty}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}:=\sup_{t}\left(t^{-1}\omega_{r,2}\left(f,t^{\frac{1}{s_{1}}},\ldots,t^{\frac{1}{s_{d}}},\mathcal{X}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{s_{i}}\right)N^{-1}\right)^{-1}\omega_{r,2}\left(f,\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{s_{i}}\right)^{\frac{1}{s_{1}}}N^{-\frac{1}{s_{1}}},\ldots,\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{s_{i}}\right)^{\frac{1}{s_{d}}}N^{-\frac{1}{s_{d}}},\mathcal{X}\right)

\displaystyle\geq N\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{s_{i}}\right)^{-1}\omega_{r,2}\left(f,h_{1}\gamma_{1},\ldots,h_{d}\gamma_{d},\mathcal{X}\right)

\displaystyle\geq N\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{s_{i}}\right)^{-1}\left\|\Delta_{\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma}}^{r}f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}.

As a result, we have
\|\Delta_{\bm{h}\odot\bm{\gamma}}^{r}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}\leq|f|_{B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}N^{-1}(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{s_{i}})
.
Plugging the above back to Eq. (
D.4
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|K_{\bm{\gamma}}*f-f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left(|f|_{B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}N^{-1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{s_{i}}\right)\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}

\displaystyle\leq|f|_{B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}^{2}N^{-2}d\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{2s_{i}}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}.

Notice that

\displaystyle C_{0}^{2}:=d\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp\left(-2\|\bm{h}\|^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}^{2s_{i}}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{h}=d\sum_{i=1}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\exp\left(-2h_{i}^{2}\right)h_{i}^{2s_{i}}dh_{i},

which is the sum of
s_{i}
-th moment of a one dimensional Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,1/4)
from
i=1
to
i=d
.
Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|K_{\bm{\gamma}}*f-f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}^{2}|f|_{B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}^{2}N^{-2}=C_{0}^{2}|f|_{B_{2,q}^{\bm{s}}(\mathcal{X})}^{2}\left(\max\{\gamma_{1}^{s_{1}},\ldots,\gamma_{d}^{s_{d}}\}\right)^{2}.

∎

Lemma D.6
.

Given a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|K_{\bm{\gamma}}\ast f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}}\leq\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\pi^{-\frac{d}{4}}(2^{r}-1)\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

Following the same arguments in the proof of proposition 4 in
Hang and Steinwart 2021
, we have
K_{\bm{\gamma}}\ast f\in\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}
.
For a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
, we define
\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}f(\mathbf{x})=f(\bm{\gamma}\odot\mathbf{x})
. We have, for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}\left(K_{\bm{\gamma}}\ast f\right)(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\bm{\gamma}}\left(\bm{\gamma}\odot\mathbf{x}-\bm{t}\right)f(\bm{t})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{1}}\left(\frac{\bm{\gamma}\odot\mathbf{x}-\bm{t}}{\bm{\gamma}}\right)\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\right)f(\bm{t})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{1}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\frac{\bm{t}}{\bm{\gamma}}\right)\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\right)f\left(\bm{t}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}K_{\mathbf{1}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\bm{t}\right)f\left(\bm{t}\odot\bm{\gamma}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{t}

\displaystyle=(K_{\mathbf{1}}\ast(\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}f))(\mathbf{x}).

Proposition 4.37 of
Steinwart and Christmann 2008
can be generalized to anisotropic case which shows that for any

f\in\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}
,
\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}f\in\mathcal{H}_{X,\mathbf{1}}
and

\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}:\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}\to\mathcal{H}_{X,\mathbf{1}}
is an isometric isomorphism. Hence we have

\displaystyle\left\|K_{\bm{\gamma}}\ast f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}\left(K_{\bm{\gamma}}\ast f\right)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\mathbf{1}}}

\displaystyle=\left\|K_{\mathbf{1}}\ast(\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}f)\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\mathbf{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\pi^{-\frac{d}{4}}(2^{r}-1)\|\tau_{\bm{\gamma}}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\pi^{-\frac{d}{4}}(2^{r}-1)\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where the second last inequality follows from
Eberts and Steinwart 2013
[Theorem 2.3] setting
\gamma=1
.
∎

Corollary D.1
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}\to\mathbb{R}
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Recall
K_{\gamma_{x}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\to\mathbb{R}
and
K_{\gamma_{o}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}\to\mathbb{R}
defined in Eq. (
66
), then we have

\displaystyle\|K_{\gamma_{x}}\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\leq C_{1}n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})},

for some constant
C_{1}
that only depends on
d_{x},d_{o},s_{x},s_{o}
.

Proof.

The proof is a direct application of
D.6
.
∎

Lemma D.7
.

Let
\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}
,
\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}
.
Let
\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\to\mathbb{R}
be an indicator function over
\{\bm{\omega}_{x}:\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}\leq\gamma_{x}^{-1}\}
and
K_{\gamma_{o}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\to\mathbb{R}
be as defined in Eq. (
66
).
Then we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}\lesssim 2^{r}n^{\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}.

Proof.

In contrast to the notation used in the rest of the document (with the exception of
D.9
), in this proof, we use
\mathcal{F}
to denote the unitary Fourier operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
, we use
\mathcal{F}_{x}
to denote the unitary Fourier operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
, and we use
\mathcal{F}_{o}
to denote the unitary Fourier operator on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})
. We let
\otimes:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})\times L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
denotes the tensor product mapping, where
(f\otimes g)(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})=f(\mathbf{x})g(\mathbf{o})
for all
(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}
. By considering a convergent sequence to
f_{1}
in
L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})\cap L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
, and similarly for
f_{2}
, we can show that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[f\otimes g]=\mathcal{F}_{x}[f]\otimes\mathcal{F}_{o}[g]

for
f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
and
g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})
. At the start of
Appendix

D
, we proved that for
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
,
f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
. Since
\|K_{\gamma_{o}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}=1

[
Giné and Nickl 2021
, Section 4.1.2]
, we have

\displaystyle\|(f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}])\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\|(f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}])(\mathbf{x},\cdot)\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\|(f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}])(\mathbf{x},\cdot)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}\|K_{\gamma_{o}}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}

\displaystyle=\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}.

where in
(a)
,
(f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}])(\mathbf{x},\cdot)
is a slice function of any representative of
f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})
, and
(b)
follows from Young’s Convolution Inequality. Hence we can apply
\mathcal{F}
to
f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
and we find

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{F}_{x}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast f\right]=(\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}\otimes\mathcal{F}_{o}[K_{\gamma_{o}}])\cdot\mathcal{F}[f].

For
\phi(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})=\exp(-\gamma_{x}^{-2}\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}^{2})\cdot\exp(-\gamma_{o}^{-2}\|\mathbf{o}\|_{2}^{2})
, its Fourier transform is

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\phi](\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})=\pi^{d_{x}/2+d_{o}/2}\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{x}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2}\right).

Hence, by definition of Gaussian RKHS norm
[
Wendland 2004
, Theorem 10.12]
,

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\mathcal{F}_{x}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}\frac{\left(\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}(\bm{\omega}_{x})\cdot\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})\cdot\mathcal{F}_{o}[K_{\gamma_{o}}](\bm{\omega}_{o})\right)^{2}}{\pi^{d_{x}/2+d_{o}/2}\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{x}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\exp(-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2})}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{o}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}}\int_{\{\bm{\omega}_{x}:\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}\leq\gamma_{x}^{-1}\}}\frac{\left(\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})\cdot\mathcal{F}_{o}[K_{\gamma_{o}}](\bm{\omega}_{o})\right)^{2}}{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{x}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot\exp(-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2})}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{o}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}\exp\left(\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\left(\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})\cdot\mathcal{F}_{o}[K_{\gamma_{o}}](\bm{\omega}_{o})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{o}.

Since
K_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}):=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\frac{1}{j^{d_{o}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}}\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\exp(-2j^{-2}\gamma_{o}^{-2}\|\mathbf{o}\|_{2}^{2})
, so we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{o}[K_{\gamma_{o}}](\bm{\omega}_{o})=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}j^{2}\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2}\right).

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\iota_{x,\gamma_{x}^{-1}}]\ast K_{\gamma_{o}}\ast f\right\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}\frac{\left(\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{r}\binom{r}{j}(-1)^{1-j}\exp(-\frac{1}{8}j^{2}\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2})\right)^{2}}{\exp(-\frac{1}{4}\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2})}d\bm{\omega}_{x}d\bm{\omega}_{o}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}2^{2r}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}\left(\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})\right)^{2}d\bm{\omega}_{x}d\bm{\omega}_{o}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{-d_{o}}2^{2r}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}^{2}.

where the last equality follows by Plancherel’s Theorem.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

Let
\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{d})\in(0,1)^{d}
. Let
k_{\bm{\gamma}}
be the anisotropic Gaussian kernel on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and let
\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}
be the RKHS associated with
k_{\bm{\gamma}}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}\exp\left(\frac{\|\bm{\omega}\odot\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2}}{4}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}=2^{d}\pi^{d/2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}\right)\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}}^{2}

Proof.

We define
\phi_{X,\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{x})=\exp(-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\gamma_{j}^{-2}x_{j}^{2})
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\phi_{X,\bm{\gamma}}](\bm{\omega})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi_{X,\bm{\gamma}}(\mathbf{x})\exp(-i\langle\mathbf{x},\bm{\omega}\rangle)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}\right)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi_{X,1}(\mathbf{x})\exp\left(-i\left\langle\mathbf{x},\bm{\omega}\odot\bm{\gamma}\right\rangle\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=\pi^{d/2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\|\bm{\omega}\odot\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2}}{4}\right)

where the last step follows from
Wendland 2004
[Theorem 5.20]. By
Kanagawa et al. 2018
[Theorem 2.4], we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{X,\bm{\gamma}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}}{\mathcal{F}[\phi_{X,\bm{\gamma}}](\bm{\omega})}d\bm{\omega}

\displaystyle=(2\pi)^{-d}\pi^{d/2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}\right)^{-1}\exp\left(\frac{\|\bm{\omega}\odot\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2}}{4}\right)\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}\;

\displaystyle=2^{-d}\pi^{-d/2}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{d}\gamma_{i}\right)^{-1}\int|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}\exp\left(\frac{\|\bm{\omega}\odot\bm{\gamma}\|_{2}^{2}}{4}\right)\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}

whence the Lemma follows.
∎

Lemma D.9
.

Suppose that
4.2
in the main text holds, and suppose
\gamma_{x}^{\delta_{x}}=\gamma_{o}^{\delta_{o}}
for some positive constant
\delta_{x},\delta_{o}
.
Suppose
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}
with
\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}\leq Cn
for some constant
C>0
. Then,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|Tf\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\gamma_{x}^{\frac{1}{8}\delta_{x}}(n\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(96)

In particular, for

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

and
\frac{1}{8}\delta_{x}=2s_{x}+d_{x}\eta_{1}+d_{x}\eta_{0}
,
\delta_{x}=\delta_{o}\frac{d_{x}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|Tf\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Proof.

Define the following sets

\displaystyle I_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\bm{\omega}_{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}:\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\gamma_{x}^{2}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{-1}\leq 1\right\}

\displaystyle\quad I_{\mathbf{o}}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\bm{\omega}_{o}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}:\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|^{2}_{2}\gamma_{o}^{2}(\log(\gamma_{o}^{-\delta_{o}}))^{-1}\leq 1\right\}

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle:=I_{\mathbf{x}}\times I_{\mathbf{o}}

\displaystyle\tilde{I}

\displaystyle:=\left\{(\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}:\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\gamma_{x}^{2}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{-1}+\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|^{2}\gamma_{o}^{2}(\log(\gamma_{o}^{-\delta_{o}}))^{-1}\leq 1\right\}.

Note that
\tilde{I}\subset I
. Let
\iota_{x}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\to\{0,1\}
denote the indicator function on
I_{\mathbf{x}}
, let
\iota_{o}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}}\to\{0,1\}
denote the indicator function on
I_{\mathbf{o}}
, let
\iota:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}\to\{0,1\}
denote the indicator function on
I
, which satisfies
\iota=\iota_{x}\cdot\iota_{o}
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\|f\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}+\left\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|T(f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota))\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+\left\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|Tf\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt+\left(1+\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\right)\left\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|Tf\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\left\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{O})}.

(97)

In the above derivations,

•

(i)
follows from
4.2
, and for
\mathcal{F}_{x},\mathcal{F}_{o}
defined in the proof of
D.7
,

\displaystyle(\forall\mathbf{o}\in\mathcal{O}),\;\mathrm{supp}(\mathcal{F}_{x}[(f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota))(\cdot,\mathbf{o})])=\mathrm{supp}(\mathcal{F}_{x}[(f\ast\mathcal{F}_{o}^{-1}[\iota_{o}])(\cdot,\mathbf{o})]\cdot\iota_{x})\subseteq I_{\mathbf{x}},

where we note that the relevant Fourier transforms exist since
f\in\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}\subset L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
.

•

(ii)
follows from a triangular inequality and a Jensen’s inequality.

Next, we bound
(\ast)=\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\|_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}
. We have

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle=\left\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\right\|_{L^{2}([0,1]^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\leq\left\|f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\left\|\mathcal{F}\left[f\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}(\iota)-f\right]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}=\|\mathcal{F}[f]\iota-\mathcal{F}[f]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{I^{c}}|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\left(\int_{\tilde{I}^{c}}|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
(a)
holds by Plancherel’s Theorem,
(b)
holds by
\tilde{I}\subseteq I
.
Recall that for
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbf{a}\odot\mathbf{b}:=(a_{1}b_{1},\ldots,a_{d}b_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. For
\bm{\omega}=(\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o})
, we proceed from above to have,

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\tilde{I}^{c}}|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}\exp\left(\frac{\|(\bm{\omega}_{x}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{o})\|^{2}_{2}}{4}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\|(\bm{\omega}_{x}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{o})\|^{2}_{2}}{4}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{\omega}\in\tilde{I}^{c}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|(\bm{\omega}_{x}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{o})\|^{2}_{2}}{8}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}}}|\mathcal{F}[f](\bm{\omega})|^{2}\exp\left(\frac{\|(\bm{\omega}_{x}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{o})\|^{2}_{2}}{4}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\sup_{\bm{\omega}\in\tilde{I}^{c}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|(\bm{\omega}_{x}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{o}\odot\bm{\gamma}_{o})\|^{2}_{2}}{8}\right)\left(\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{XO,\bm{\gamma}_{x},\bm{\gamma}_{o}}}^{2}2^{-\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}}{2}}\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}\gamma_{x}^{2}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{-1}+\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|^{2}\gamma_{o}^{2}(\log(\gamma_{o}^{-\delta_{o}}))^{-1}\geq 1}\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma_{x}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{x}\|_{2}^{2}+\gamma_{o}^{2}\|\bm{\omega}_{o}\|_{2}^{2}}{8}\right)\left(n\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}})\right)\left(n\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\asymp\gamma_{x}^{\frac{1}{8}\delta_{x}}\left(n\gamma_{x}^{d_{x}}\gamma_{o}^{d_{o}}\right)^{1/2},

where
(c)
holds by Lemma
D.8
.
Now we have proved the first claim of this lemma. Next, we proceed to prove the second claim by plugging in the specific values of
\delta_{x},\delta_{o}
and
\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}
.
Recall that
\frac{1}{8}\delta_{x}=2s_{x}+d_{x}\eta_{1}+d_{x}\eta_{0}+d_{x}
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\ast)\lesssim n^{-\frac{\frac{\delta_{x}}{8}\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}\cdot n^{\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}=n^{\frac{-\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}-\eta_{0}-1}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

We plug it back to Eq. (
97
) and obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|Tf\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log(\gamma_{x}^{-\delta_{x}}))^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}n^{\frac{-\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}-\eta_{0}-1}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|Tf\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+1}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma_{x}^{-d_{x}\eta_{0}}(\log n)^{\frac{d_{x}\eta_{0}}{2}}\|Tf\|_{L^{2}(P_{ZO})}+n^{-\frac{\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}}{1+2(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

So the proof is concluded.
∎

Lemma D.10
.

Suppose
2.2
hold, and let

\displaystyle\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},\quad\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Then, for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\|C_{FO}\|\geq a\left(\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{4}\right)^{\frac{d_{x}+d_{o}}{2}}n^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}.

Proof.

By definition of the operator norm, we have

\displaystyle\quad\|C_{FO}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}=1}\left\langle f,\E[(F_{\ast}(Z,O)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(O))\otimes(F_{\ast}(Z,O)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(O))]f\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}=1}\E\left[\left(\E[f(X,O)|Z,O]\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}=1}\left(\E_{ZO\sim P_{ZO}}\left[\E[f(X,O)|Z,O]\right]\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\|f\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}=1}\E[f(X,O)]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\mu_{XO}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2},

where
\mu_{XO}:=\E_{P_{XO}}[\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(X)\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(O)]
.
Notice that

\displaystyle\|\mu_{XO}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\iint_{\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{X}}\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o})p_{XO}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\iint_{\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{X}}\phi_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\otimes\phi_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o}^{\prime})p_{XO}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{o}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}\right\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{X}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}\times\mathcal{X}}K_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o},\mathbf{o}^{\prime})K_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})p_{XO}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})p_{XO}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\mathbf{o}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}a^{2}\int_{[1/4,3/4]^{d_{o}}}\int_{[1/4,3/4]^{d_{o}}}K_{\gamma_{o}}(\mathbf{o},\mathbf{o}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}^{\prime}\int_{[1/4,3/4]^{d_{x}}}\int_{[1/4,3/4]^{d_{x}}}K_{\gamma_{x}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}a^{2}\left(\gamma_{x}\sqrt{\pi}\left[\frac{\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma_{x}}\right)}{2}+\gamma_{x}\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4\gamma_{x}^{2}}\right)-1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\right]\right)^{d_{x}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\gamma_{o}\sqrt{\pi}\left[\frac{\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma_{o}}\right)}{2}+\gamma_{o}\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4\gamma_{o}^{2}}\right)-1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\right]\right)^{d_{o}},

where
\mathrm{erf}(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int^{x}_{-x}\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)\;\mathrm{d}t

is the standard error function of normal distribution. Step
(a)
holds by
2.2
and step
(b)
holds by using
D.11
. We plug in
\gamma_{x}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{d_{x}}}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}
,
\gamma_{o}=n^{-\frac{\frac{1}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}}
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\mu_{XO}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\gamma_{x},\gamma_{o}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\geq}}a^{2}\left(\sqrt{\pi}\gamma_{x}/4\right)^{d_{x}}\left(\sqrt{\pi}\gamma_{o}/4\right)^{d_{o}}=a^{2}(\sqrt{\pi}/4)^{d_{x}+d_{o}}n^{-\frac{1+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}{1+(2+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}})(\frac{s_{x}}{d_{x}}+\eta_{1})}},

where
(i)
holds for sufficiently large
n\geq 1
because
\mathrm{erf}(x)\geq\frac{1}{2}
when
x\geq 1
.
∎

Lemma D.11
.

Let
k_{\gamma}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be the Gaussian kernel with length scale
\gamma\in(0,1]
. Then we have,

\displaystyle\int_{[1/4,3/4]^{d}}\int_{[1/4,3/4]^{d}}k_{\gamma}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}=\left(\gamma\sqrt{\pi}\left[\frac{\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)}{2}+\gamma\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4\gamma^{2}}\right)-1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\right]\right)^{d_{x}}.

Here,
\mathrm{erf}(x):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int^{x}_{-x}\exp\left(-t^{2}\right)\;\mathrm{d}t
is the standard error function.

Proof.

Since
k_{\gamma}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\prod_{i=1}^{d}\exp\left(-\frac{(x_{i}-x_{i}^{\prime})^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)
, it suffices to prove the following result

\displaystyle\int_{1/4}^{3/4}\int_{1/4}^{3/4}k_{\gamma}(x_{i},x_{i}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}x_{i}\mathrm{d}x_{i}^{\prime}=\gamma^{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left[\frac{\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)}{2\gamma}+\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4\gamma^{2}}\right)-1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\right].

Notice that

\displaystyle\int_{1/4}^{3/4}\exp\left(-\frac{(x_{i}-x_{i}^{\prime})^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\;\mathrm{d}x_{i}

\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{(1/4-x_{i}^{\prime})/\gamma}^{(3/4-x_{i}^{\prime})/\gamma}\exp(-x^{2})\;\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\gamma\int^{(3/4-x_{i}^{\prime})/\gamma}_{-\infty}\exp(-x^{2})\;\mathrm{d}x-\gamma\int^{(1/4-x_{i}^{\prime})/\gamma}_{-\infty}\exp(-x^{2})\;\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\gamma\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}\left(1+\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{3/4-x_{i}^{\prime}}{\gamma}\right)\right)-\gamma\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}\left(1-\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{x_{i}^{\prime}-1/4}{\gamma}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\gamma\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}\left(\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{3/4-x_{i}^{\prime}}{\gamma}\right)+\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{x_{i}^{\prime}-1/4}{\gamma}\right)\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\int_{1/4}^{3/4}\int_{1/4}^{3/4}\exp\left(-\frac{(x_{i}-x_{i}^{\prime})^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\;\mathrm{d}x_{i}\;\mathrm{d}x_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\gamma\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}\int_{1/4}^{3/4}\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{3/4-x_{i}^{\prime}}{\gamma}\right)+\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{x_{i}^{\prime}-1/4}{\gamma}\right)\;\mathrm{d}x_{i}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\sqrt{\pi}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2\gamma}}\mathrm{erf}(y)\;\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\sqrt{\pi}\left[\frac{\mathrm{erf}\left(\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)}{2\gamma}+\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{1}{4\gamma^{2}}\right)-1}{\sqrt{\pi}}\right].

where the last inequality holds by using the identity

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{erf}(x)dx=x\cdot\mathrm{erf}(x)+e^{-x^{2}}/\sqrt{\pi}+C.

∎

Appendix E
Proof of Theorem
4.2
in the main text

E.1
Relationship Between the NPIR-O Model and the NPIV-O Model

Following
Chen and Reiss 2011
, we first establish that the NPIV-O model is no more informative than the reduced form nonparametric indirect regression with observed confounders (NPIR-O) model.

Definition 16

(Restricted NPIV-O model)
.

Let
\sigma_{0}>0
be a finite constant. Recall
\mathfrak{S}
as defined in
2.3
in the main text. Let
\mathcal{C}
be a set of elements
(P_{\epsilon ZXO},f)
such that the following property holds:
(\forall f\in\mathfrak{S})\;(\exists P_{\epsilon ZXO},f)\in\mathcal{C}
such that
P_{ZY}
is determined by
P_{\epsilon ZX}
and
f
, and that

\displaystyle Y_{i}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i}\mid Z=\mathbf{z}_{i},O=\mathbf{o}_{i}]=f(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})+\epsilon_{i}-(Tf)(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})

given
Z_{i}=\mathbf{z}_{i},O_{i}=\mathbf{o}_{i}
is
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i}))
-distributed with
\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\geq\sigma_{0}^{2}
.

For an NPIV-O model as defined in
16
, we specify the reduced form NPIR-O model as

\displaystyle Y_{i}=(Tf)(Z_{i},O_{i})+\upsilon_{i},\quad i=1,\dots,n

with
(Z_{i},O_{i},\upsilon_{i})
i.i.d.,
P_{\upsilon_{i}\mid Z_{i}=\mathbf{z}_{i},O_{i}=\mathbf{o}_{i}}=\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i}))
,
f\in\mathfrak{S}
the unknown structural function, and
T:L^{2}(P_{XO})\to L^{2}(P_{ZO})
a known operator satisfying
4.1
. The observations corresponding to the NPIR are
\{(Y_{i},\mathbf{z}_{i},\mathbf{o}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
.

Definition 17

(NPIR-O model class)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be as defined in
16
. The NPIR-O model class
\mathcal{C}_{0}
consists of all model parameters
(P_{Z^{\prime}O^{\prime}},\sigma(\cdot,\cdot),f)
such that
(\exists(P_{\epsilon ZX},f)\in\mathcal{C})
with the following properties:
P_{ZO}=P_{Z^{\prime}O^{\prime}}
,
\sigma^{2}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\geq\sigma_{0}^{2}>0
, the conditional law
P_{X\mid Z,O}
is prescribed according to
T
, and
P_{\epsilon\mid ZOX}
is arbitrary among the conditions imposed in
\mathcal{C}
.

The following Lemma is
Chen 2007
, by augmenting relevant variables to include observed confounders
O
.

Lemma E.1
.

The NPIR-O model is more informative than the NPIV-O model in the sense that for each estimator
\hat{f}_{n}
for the NPIV-O model, there is an estimator
\tilde{f}_{n}
for the NPIR-O model with

\displaystyle\sup_{(P_{ZO},\sigma(\cdot,\cdot),f)\in\mathcal{C}_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{(P_{ZO},\sigma(\cdot,\cdot),f)}[\|\tilde{f}_{n}-f\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}]\leq\sup_{(P_{\epsilon ZXO},f)\in\mathcal{C}}\mathbb{E}_{(P_{\epsilon ZXO,f})}[\|\hat{f}_{n}-f\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}].

In this section, we provide a lower bound for the NPIR-O model class defined in
17
, which by the above discussion implies a lower bound for the (restricted) NPIV-O model class defined in
16
.

E.2
The Lower Bound for NPIR-O Model

Step One.

We take
\mathfrak{m}
to be the smallest even integer such that
\mathfrak{m}>s_{x}\vee s_{o}
.
To help us construct
f_{\mathbf{v}}
, we need to introduce several functions.
We define

\displaystyle M_{\mathfrak{K},\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}(\mathbf{x}):=\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\iota_{\mathfrak{m}}\left(2^{\left\lfloor\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}\right\rfloor}x_{i}+\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}\right),\quad M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o}):=\prod_{j=1}^{d_{o}}\iota_{\mathfrak{m}}\left(2^{\left\lfloor\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{o}}\right\rfloor}o_{i}-\ell_{o,j}\right).

(98)

Define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}:=\left\{({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}):{\bm{\ell}}_{x}\in\left\{0,1,\dots,\left\lfloor\frac{0.8\pi}{\zeta}2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\right\rfloor\right\}^{d_{x}},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}\in\left(m\mathbb{Z}\cap\left\{1,\dots,2^{\left\lfloor\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{o}}\right\rfloor}\right\}\right)^{d_{o}}\right\},

(99)

then we can compute the size of
\mathcal{L}
as

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}|\asymp\zeta^{-d_{x}}2^{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(\frac{d_{x}}{s_{x}}+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}\right)}

(100)

For
({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})\in\mathcal{L}
, define
\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}
as an indicator function of the set

\displaystyle I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}:=\bigtimes_{j=1}^{d_{x}}\left[1.1\pi+\zeta\ell_{x,j}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}},1.1\pi+(\zeta\ell_{x,j}+1)2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\right],

(101)

Next, we define

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(\mathbf{x}):=\left(M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\right)\left(2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\mathbf{x}\right),\quad\Omega_{\mathfrak{K}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{o})

\displaystyle:=\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(\mathbf{x})M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o}).

(102)

Now we are ready to define
f_{\mathbf{v}}
. Note that a vector
\mathbf{v}\in\{0,1\}^{|\mathcal{L}|}
canonically associates to each point
({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})
a value
\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\in\{0,1\}
. We define

\displaystyle f_{\mathbf{v}}:=\epsilon_{0}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\Omega_{\mathfrak{K}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})},

(103)

where
\epsilon_{0}>0
is a fixed scaler to be chosen later, to ensure that
\|f_{\mathbf{v}}\|_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\leq 1
, thus ensuring that
f_{\mathbf{v}}
satisfies
2.3
. The function class
\mathfrak{F}:=\{f_{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{v}\in\{0,1\}^{|\mathcal{L}|}\}
.
For each
f_{\mathbf{v}}
, consider the joint data generating distribution
P_{ZXOY}
specified as follows:

1.

The marginal distribution
P_{ZO}
is the product of independent distributions
P_{Z}
and
P_{O}
, where both are uniform distributions on
[0,1]^{d_{z}}
and
[0,1]^{d_{o}}
.

2.

The marginal distribution
P_{XO}
is the product of independent distributions
P_{X}
and
P_{O}
, where
P_{O}
is the uniform distribution on
[0,1]^{d_{o}}
, and
P_{X}
is supported on
[-1/2,1/2]^{d_{x}}
and admits the following density function:

\displaystyle p_{X}(\mathbf{x})\propto\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}g(x_{i}),\quad g(x_{i}):=\exp\left(-\frac{2}{1-4x_{i}^{2}}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{x_{i}\in[-1/2,1/2]}.

(104)

3.

The conditional distribution
P_{X\mid Z,O}
satisfies
2.2
and it induces an operator
T:L^{2}\left(P_{XO}\right)\rightarrow L^{2}\left(P_{ZO}\right)
that satisfies
4.3
.

4.

The conditional distribution
P_{Y\mid Z=\mathbf{z},O=\mathbf{o}}
is a Gaussian distribution
\mathcal{N}((Tf_{\mathbf{v}})(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}),\sigma^{2})
for any
\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathbf{o}\in\mathcal{O}
.

Step Two.

Now we are going to present some properties of the basis
\Omega_{\mathfrak{K}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}
, which will be used later on to prove some properties of
f_{\mathbf{v}}
. By Young’s Convolution Theorem, we have

\displaystyle\left\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\leq\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\|\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}<\infty,

hence its Fourier transform is well-defined. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}](\bm{\omega}_{x})

\displaystyle=2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\mathcal{F}\left[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\ast\mathcal{F}^{-1}[\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\right]\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\cdot\mathcal{F}\left[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\right]\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}\right)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\left[M_{\mathfrak{K},\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\right]\left(\bm{\omega}_{x}\right)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}\right).

(105)

Also, since
\mathcal{F}[\iota_{\mathfrak{m}}](\omega)=\exp{(-\mathfrak{m}i\omega/2)}\cdot\sin(\omega/2)^{\mathfrak{m}}\cdot(\omega/2)^{-\mathfrak{m}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}\left[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\right](\bm{\omega}_{x})=\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\frac{\sin(\omega_{i,x}/2)^{\mathfrak{m}}}{(\omega_{i,x}/2)^{\mathfrak{m}}}.

(106)

Now we can see that we pick the location vector to be
-\bm{\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}
such that the Fourier transform of
M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}
is a real valued function. Note that since
\zeta\geq 1
, we have
(\zeta\ell_{x,j}+1)2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\leq\zeta(\ell_{x,j}+1)2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}
for
j\in\{1,\ldots,d_{x}\}
, hence
I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\cap I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}^{\prime}}=\emptyset
if
{\bm{\ell}}_{x}\neq{\bm{\ell}}_{x}^{\prime}
which means that the support of
\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]
is disjoint for
{\bm{\ell}}_{x}\neq{\bm{\ell}}_{x}^{\prime}
.
Also, note that

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}(M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}})=\bigtimes_{j=1}^{d_{o}}[\ell_{o,j}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{o}}},(\ell_{o,j}+\mathfrak{m})2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{o}}}]

and
\ell_{o,j}
are multiples of
\mathfrak{m}
by definition of
\mathcal{L}
in Eq. (
99
), we have
\mathrm{supp}(M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}})\cap\mathrm{supp}(M_{k,{\bm{\ell}}_{o}^{\prime}})=\emptyset
for any
{\bm{\ell}}_{o}\neq{\bm{\ell}}_{o}^{\prime}
.

Step Three.

In this step, we are going to prove that
f_{\mathbf{v}}\in B_{2,\infty}^{s_{x},s_{o}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
and its Besov norm is bounded by
\epsilon_{0}
.
We have,
(\forall\mathbf{o}\in\mathcal{O})
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[f_{\mathbf{v}}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})](\bm{\omega}_{x})=\epsilon_{0}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}(\cdot,\mathbf{o})](\bm{\omega}_{x})

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}](\bm{\omega}_{x})\cdot M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o})

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}]\left(2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}\right)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}\right)\cdot M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o})

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\mathcal{F}[M_{\mathfrak{K},\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}](\bm{\omega}_{x})\cdot M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o})\cdot\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}\right)\right).

(107)

Note that, for fixed
{\bm{\ell}}_{o}
,
\bm{\omega}_{x}\mapsto\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x})
is the indicator function on

\displaystyle\bigcup_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}:\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}>0}\bigtimes_{j=1}^{d_{x}}\left[1.1\pi 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}+\zeta\ell_{x,j},1.1\pi 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}+(\zeta\ell_{x,j}+1)\right],

The above observation as applied to the right hand side of Eq. (
E.2
) means that we can apply
E.3
to obtain

\displaystyle\|f_{\mathbf{v}}\|_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{0}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\cdot 2^{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}=\epsilon_{0}.

(108)

Hence we can choose
\epsilon_{0}
to be a small scalar such that
\|f_{\mathbf{v}}\|_{B^{s_{x},s_{o}}_{2,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})}\leq 1
. The rest of the conditions that
f_{\mathbf{v}}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})\cap L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})\cap C^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{o}})
are all trivial to verify. Therefore,
f_{\mathbf{v}}
satisfies
2.3
.

Step Four.

By the Gilbert-Varshamov Bound
Tsybakov 2008
[Lemma 2.9], for
|\mathcal{L}|\geq 8
, there exists a subset
V_{\mathfrak{K}}
of
\{0,1\}^{|\mathcal{L}|}
such that
\bm{0}\in V_{\mathfrak{K}}
and

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left|\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}-\beta_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\right|^{2}\geq\frac{|\mathcal{L}|}{8}

(109)

for
\mathbf{v}\neq\mathbf{v}^{\prime}\in V_{\mathfrak{K}}
and
|V_{\mathfrak{K}}|\geq 2^{\frac{|\mathcal{L}|}{8}}
.
Note that since
\sqrt{p_{X}}
is an even function over
[-1/2,1/2]^{d_{x}}
, its Fourier transform
q
is a real valued function. Define

\displaystyle q(\bm{\omega}_{x}):=\mathcal{F}[\sqrt{p_{X}}](\bm{\omega}_{x}),\quad\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x}):=\mathcal{F}[\sqrt{p_{X}}](\bm{\omega}_{x})1_{[-\zeta/3,\zeta/3]^{d_{x}}}(\bm{\omega}_{x}).

(110)

For coefficients
\alpha_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\Omega_{\mathfrak{K}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{d_{o}}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}\\
\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{x},\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}(\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}},\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{o})}\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(\mathbf{x})\Omega_{k\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{x}}(\mathbf{x})M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o})M_{k\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o})p_{X}(\mathbf{x})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}\\
\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{x},\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\end{subarray}}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}(\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}},\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{o})}\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(\mathbf{x})\Omega_{k\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{x}}(\mathbf{x})p_{X}(\mathbf{x})\left(\int_{[0,1]^{d_{o}}}M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o})M_{k\tilde{{\bm{\ell}}}_{o}}(\mathbf{o})\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{o}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\|M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(\mathbf{x})\sqrt{p(\mathbf{x})}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\|M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\mathcal{F}\left[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\sqrt{p}\right](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}\|M_{0\bm{0}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\alpha_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\mathcal{F}\left[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\sqrt{p}\right](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}.

(111)

where
(a)
follows from the fact that
M_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}
have pairwise disjoint support verified in
Step Two
above; and
(b)
follows from the Plancherel’s Theorem. By Eq. (
111
), we have

\displaystyle\quad\|f_{\mathbf{v}}-f_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}}\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}\|M_{0\bm{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}-\beta_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\right)\mathcal{F}\left[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\sqrt{p_{X}}\right](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon_{0}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}\|M_{0\bm{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}^{2}\left((A)^{2}/2-(B)^{2}\right),

(112)

where the last step follows from the reverse triangular inequality
(a+b)^{2}\geq\frac{a^{2}}{2}-b^{2}
, and we define

\displaystyle(A)^{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}-\beta_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\right)\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}

\displaystyle(B)^{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}-\beta_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\right)\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast(q-\tilde{q})\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}.

In order to lower bound Eq. (
E.2
), we need to lower bound
(A)^{2}
and upper bound
(B)^{2}
.
First, we are going to lower bound
(A)^{2}
.
From Eq. (
105
), we know that the support of
\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]
is

\displaystyle\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}:=\bigtimes_{j=1}^{d_{x}}\left[1.1\pi 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}+\zeta\ell_{x,j},1.1\pi 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}+(\zeta\ell_{x,j}+1)\right].

(113)

Since the support of
\tilde{q}
is
[-\zeta/3,\zeta/3]^{d_{x}}
, by the standard fact that
\mathrm{supp}(f\ast g)\subseteq\mathrm{supp}(f)+\mathrm{supp}(g)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}\left(\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right)

\displaystyle\subseteq\bigtimes_{j=1}^{d_{x}}\left[1.1\pi 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}+\zeta(\ell_{x,j}-1/3),1.1\pi 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}+(\zeta(\ell_{x,j}+1/3)+1)\right]

\displaystyle=:\Lambda_{\mathfrak{K}}

(114)

Note that for
\zeta\geq 3
, we have
\zeta(\ell_{j}+1/3)+1\leq\zeta(\ell_{j}+1-1/3)
, hence
\mathrm{supp}\left(\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right)
is pairwise disjoint with respect to different
{\bm{\ell}}_{x}
.
Hence we obtain that

\displaystyle(A)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}-\beta_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})})^{2}\left\|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}.

Notice that

\displaystyle\quad\left\|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{K}}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}](\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle=2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\int_{\Lambda_{\mathfrak{K}}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}]\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)\cdot\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\geq}}2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\int_{\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}]\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)\cdot\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\int_{\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}}\left|\int_{\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}}\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}]\left(2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)\cdot\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}.

(115)

(i)
above holds because
\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}\subset\Lambda_{\mathfrak{K}}
; and
(ii)
holds because of the indicator function.
Notice, for any
\bm{\omega}_{x},\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\in\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}
, we have for
j\in\{1,\ldots,d_{x}\}
,
1\geq\bm{\omega}_{x,j}-\bm{\omega}^{\prime}_{x,j}\geq-1
.
Since for any
-1\leq t\leq 1
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,d_{x}\}
and
g
defined in Eq. (
104
), there is
\mathcal{F}[\sqrt{g}](t)=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\sqrt{g}(x_{i})\exp(-x_{i}t)dx_{i}=\int_{-1/2}^{1/2}\sqrt{g}(x_{i})\cos(x_{i}t)dx_{i}>0
.
So we have, for
\zeta>3
,

\displaystyle\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})=q(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})=\mathcal{F}[\sqrt{p_{X}}](\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})=\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\mathcal{F}[\sqrt{g}](\bm{\omega}_{x,i}-\bm{\omega}^{\prime}_{x,i})>0.

Since both
\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}](2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})
and
\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime})
are positive and real, we continue from Eq. (
E.2
) to have

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\left(\inf_{\bm{\omega}_{x}\in I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right)^{2}\cdot\underbrace{\int_{\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}}\left(\int_{\tilde{I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}}\tilde{q}\left(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}}_{(\ast)}.

Notice that in the integration in the term
(\ast)
above, only the difference of
\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}
show up.
So we can obtain

\displaystyle(\ast)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{d_{x}}}\left(\int_{[0,1]^{d_{x}}}\tilde{q}\left(\bm{\omega}_{x}-\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\tilde{q}_{i}\left(\bm{\omega}_{x,i}-\bm{\omega}_{x,i}^{\prime}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{\bm{\omega}_{x,i}-1}^{\bm{\omega}_{x,i}}\tilde{q}_{i}\left(\bm{\omega}_{x,i}^{\prime\prime}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{\bm{\omega}_{x,i}-1}^{\bm{\omega}_{x,i}}q_{i}\left(\bm{\omega}_{x,i}^{\prime\prime}\right)\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}^{\prime\prime}\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}.

The second last equality holds by change of variables, and the last equality holds because
\tilde{q}_{i}(\omega)=q_{i}(\omega)
for
\omega\in[-\zeta/3,\zeta/3]
.
So
(\ast)
is a strictly positive constant independent of
\zeta,k,n
. Plugging it back to above, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}\geq 2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\left(\inf_{\bm{\omega}_{x}\in I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right)^{2}\cdot(\ast).

Notice that, since
I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\subseteq[1.1\pi,1.95\pi]^{d_{x}}
and we use Eq. (
106
) to obtain, since
M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}
is an even function and thus has real-valued Fourier transform

\displaystyle\inf_{\bm{\omega}_{x}\in I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}\left|\mathcal{F}[M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right|^{2}=\inf_{\bm{\omega}_{x}\in I_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}}\left|\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\frac{\sin(\bm{\omega}_{x,i}/2)^{\mathfrak{m}}}{(\bm{\omega}_{x,i}/2)^{\mathfrak{m}}}\right|^{2}\geq\inf_{\omega\in[1.1\pi,1.95\pi]}\left(\frac{\sin(\omega/2)}{\omega/2}\right)^{2\mathfrak{m}d_{x}}>0.

Define the following positive constant, independent of
\zeta
,
k
or
n
,

\displaystyle C_{\chi}:=(\ast)\cdot\inf_{\omega\in[1.1\pi,1.95\pi]}\left(\frac{\sin(\omega/2)}{\omega/2}\right)^{2\mathfrak{m}d_{x}}.

(116)

We thus have

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}\geq C_{\chi}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\frac{d_{x}}{s_{x}}}.

(117)

Therefore,

\displaystyle(A)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}(\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}-\beta_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}({\bm{\ell}}_{x}^{\prime},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}^{\prime})})^{2}C_{\chi}2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\geq}}\frac{C_{\chi}}{8}|\mathcal{L}|2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\gtrsim}}\zeta^{-d_{x}}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(\frac{d_{x}}{s_{x}}-\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}\right)}.

where
(a)
follows from Eq. (
109
) and
(b)
follows from Eq. (
100
).

Next, we are going to upper bound
(B)
.
We have

\displaystyle(B)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left\|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast(q-\tilde{q})\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\|q-\tilde{q}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}\|q-\tilde{q}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\|M_{\mathfrak{K},\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{=}}\|q-\tilde{q}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{2s_{x}}}\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\|q-\tilde{q}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}1\right)^{2}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|q-\tilde{q}\|^{2}_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\zeta^{-2d_{x}}2^{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(\frac{2d_{x}}{s_{x}}+\frac{d_{o}}{s_{o}}\right)}2^{-\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}\|q-\tilde{q}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}\zeta^{-\frac{1}{2}d_{x}}2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}

(118)

In the above derivations,
(a)
holds by Young’s convolution inequality,
(b)
holds by Plancherel’s Theorem and Eq. (
105
),
(c)
holds by the change of variables
\mathbf{x}\leftarrow 2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\mathbf{x}
, and
(d)
holds for
\zeta\geq 1
.
We have

\displaystyle\|q-\tilde{q}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\left|q(\bm{\omega}_{x})-\tilde{q}(\bm{\omega}_{x})\right|\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}2^{d_{x}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\int_{\zeta/3}^{\infty}\left|\mathcal{F}[\sqrt{g}](\bm{\omega}_{x,i})\right|\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\asymp}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\int_{\zeta/3}^{\infty}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}^{-3/4}\exp(-\sqrt{\bm{\omega}_{x,i}})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\leq\zeta^{-\frac{3}{4}d_{x}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\int_{\zeta}^{\infty}\exp(-\sqrt{\bm{\omega}_{x,i}})\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x,i}

\displaystyle=\zeta^{-\frac{3}{4}d_{x}}2^{d_{x}}\left(1+\zeta^{1/2}\right)^{d_{x}}\exp\left(-d_{x}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2d_{x}}\zeta^{-\frac{1}{4}d_{x}}\exp\left(-d_{x}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

(119)

where in
(i)
we use the definition of
\tilde{q}
in Eq. (
110
); and in
(ii)

we use the asymptotic decay of the Fourier transform of the bump function
[
Johnson 2015
, Section 2]
.

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}[\sqrt{g}](\omega)\right|\asymp\omega^{-3/4}\exp(-\sqrt{\omega}),\quad\omega\gg 1.

(120)

Hence, we plug Eq. (
119
) back to Eq. (
118
) and we find that

\displaystyle(B)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\zeta^{-\frac{1}{2}d_{x}}2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}+\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\left(2^{2d_{x}}\zeta^{-\frac{1}{4}d_{x}}\exp\left(-d_{x}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right)^{2}\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}+\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\zeta^{-d_{x}}\exp\left(-2d_{x}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right).

(121)

Hence in order for
(B)^{2}\leq(A)^{2}/4
to hold, a sufficient condition on
\zeta
is given by the following inequality, up to some constants,

\displaystyle 2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}+\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\zeta^{-d_{x}}\exp\left(-2d_{x}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{4}\zeta^{-d_{x}}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}

\displaystyle\iff\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\exp\left(-2d_{x}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)\lesssim 2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}.

(122)

Hence for sufficiently large
\zeta=\zeta(n)
, such that Eq. (
122
) is satisfied, we have
(A)\geq(B)/2
, thus

\displaystyle\|f_{\mathbf{v}}-f_{\mathbf{v}^{\prime}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}\|M_{0,\bm{-\frac{\mathfrak{m}}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}^{2}\frac{1}{4}(A)^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}\zeta^{-d_{x}}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}\zeta^{-d_{x}}.

(123)

Step Five

Recall the distributions
P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}}
defined above, and recall that
\bm{0}\in V_{\mathfrak{K}}
for
V_{\mathfrak{K}}
defined in Eq. (
109
).
In this step, we are going to show that, for
P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}}^{\otimes n}:=P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}}\otimes\dots\otimes P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}}
which is a probability distribution over
(\mathcal{Z}\times\mathcal{O}\times\mathbb{R})^{n}
, it satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|V_{\mathfrak{K}}|}\sum_{\mathbf{v}\in V_{\mathfrak{K}}}\operatorname{KL}\left(P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}}^{\otimes n},P_{f_{\bm{0}}}^{\otimes n}\right)\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{2}n2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}\eta_{1}-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}}.

Notice that KL divergence tensorizes over independent copies at each dimension, we have that
\operatorname{KL}(P_{f_{\bm{0}}}^{\otimes n}\|P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}}^{\otimes n})=n\operatorname{KL}(P_{f_{\bm{0}}}\|P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}})
, so we are going to study
\operatorname{KL}(P_{f_{\bm{0}}}\|P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}})
as follows.

\displaystyle\operatorname{KL}\left(P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}},P_{f_{\bm{0}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\E_{(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o})\sim P_{ZO}}\left[\operatorname{KL}\left(P_{f_{\mathbf{v}}}\left(\cdot\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}\right)\right),P_{f_{\bm{0}}}\left(\cdot\mid\mathbf{z},\mathbf{o}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{=}}\frac{\left\|Tf_{\mathbf{v}}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(P_{ZO}\right)}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}\eta_{1}}\frac{\left\|f_{\mathbf{v}}\right\|_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}

(124)

In the above chain of derivations,
we use
Blanchard and Mücke 2018
[Proposition 6.2] in
(a)
; and in
(b)
we use the
4.3
along with the fact from Eq. (
E.2
) that the support of the Fourier transform of
f_{\mathbf{v}}
is indeed in high frequency.

\displaystyle\mathrm{supp}(\mathcal{F}[f_{\mathbf{v}}])=\cup_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o}\in\mathcal{L}}\tilde{I}_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\subseteq\left[1.1\pi\cdot 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}},1.9\pi\cdot 2^{\mathfrak{K}\frac{\underline{s}}{s_{x}}}\right]^{d_{x}}.

Next notice that, by Eq. (
111
), we have

\displaystyle\|f_{\mathbf{v}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(P_{XO})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}\|M_{0,\bm{0}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\beta_{\mathbf{v}({\bm{\ell}}_{x},{\bm{\ell}}_{o})}\mathcal{F}\left[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\sqrt{p}\right](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}\|M_{0,\bm{0}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{o}})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{F}\left[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\sqrt{p}\right](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right|\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\lesssim}}\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}}\left(\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{F}\left[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\sqrt{p}\right](\bm{\omega}_{x})\right|\right)^{2}\;\mathrm{d}\bm{\omega}_{x}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast(q-\tilde{q}+\tilde{q})\right|\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}\epsilon_{0}^{2}2^{-2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}\left(1-\frac{d_{x}}{2s_{x}}\right)}\left(2\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast(q-\tilde{q})\right|\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}+2\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right|\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}\right).

(125)

In the above derivations,
(a)
holds by Eq. (
111
),
(b)
holds by
\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{o}}1\lesssim 2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{o}}{s_{o}}}
,
(c)
holds by triangular inequality.
The first term in Eq. (
E.2
) has already been upper bounded in Eq. (
E.2
). Hence for sufficiently large
\zeta
that satisfies Eq. (
122
),

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast(q-\tilde{q})\right|\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}\zeta^{-\frac{d_{x}}{2}}\exp\left(-2d_{x}\zeta^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}2^{-\frac{2\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}=2^{-\frac{\mathfrak{K}\underline{s}d_{x}}{s_{x}}}.

The second term in Eq. (
E.2
) can be upper bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}\left|\mathcal{F}[\Omega_{\mathfrak{K},{\bm{\ell}}_{x}}]\ast\tilde{q}\right|\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}})}
